### 4. MINING & TOOLS

**File Path:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mining.ts`

**Tool Tiers & Requirements:**

Mining respects a **mineTier** system (`canMineBlock` function):
- Tier 0 (bare hand): grass, dirt, sand, leaves, glass, farmland, wheat, cactus, bed, torches, doors, planks, wood, brick
- Tier 1 (wood pickaxe+): stone, cobblestone, brick
- Tier 2 (stone pickaxe+): sliver ore
- Tier 3 (sliver/gold pickaxe+): ruby ore, gold ore
- Tier 4 (sapphire/diamond pickaxe+): sapphire ore, diamond ore

**Mining Mechanics:**
- **Mining Rate:** 2.1 progress/sec × tool's minePower; bare hand has 0.8 power
- **Mine Reach:** 7 blocks (cast from eye position)
- **Durability Loss:** 1 per block broken (tools/weapons degrade and break at 0 durability)
- **Block Drops:** Via `rollBlockDrops()` function — most blocks drop their item equivalent via `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` map; grass has 20% chance to also drop seeds; mature wheat (Stage3) drops wheat + 1-2 seeds
- **Chest Breaking:** Spills all contents into inventory on break (all-or-nothing; refunded if full)
- **Door Breaking:** Breaks both lower and upper halves atomically
- **Furnace:** Breaking destroys it (no smelting inventory persistence yet)
- **Unmineable:** Bedrock, air, spawner (hardness 30)

**Pickaxe Progression:**
```
wood (1.05 power, tier 1) → stone (1.55, tier 2) → sliver (2.2, tier 3) → 
ruby (2.8, tier 4) → sapphire (3.3, tier 5) → gold (3.8, tier 6) → diamond (4.4, tier 7)
```

---

### 5. INVENTORY & HOTBAR

**File Path:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts`

**Inventory Structure:**
- **Hotbar Slots:** 9 slots (displayed at bottom, directly selectable)
- **Inventory Slots:** 36 total (9 hotbar + 27 main grid)
- **Max Stack Size:** 99 items per slot (stackable items only; tools/weapons/armor are 1 per slot, each tracks own durability)
- **Chest Slots:** 27 slots per placed chest (3×9 grid, persisted as block-entity)

**Inventory UI Components:**

**Hotbar** (`/components/game/Hotbar.tsx`):
- 9-slot row at bottom of screen
- Selection outline (white border) highlights current slot
- Selected item name fades in above bar
- Click to switch slots (keyboard keys 1-9 also switch)

**Inventory Panel** (`/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`):
- **Armor Column** (left): 6 equippable slots (helmet, face_mask, neck_protection, chestplate, leggings, boots)
- **Storage Grid** (center): 3×9 grid of 27 main inventory slots
- **Chest Grid** (conditional): 3×9 grid of 27 chest slots (when chest is open)
- **Hotbar Row** (bottom of panel): Same 9 hotbar slots for visibility
- **Recipe Book** (right): All 48 recipes displayed with ingredient icons and result icon; greyed out if ingredients insufficient; click to craft

**Inventory Mechanics:**
- Click a slot, then click another to swap contents (click-to-select, click-to-swap pattern)
- Armor items: click to equip/unequip (toggle in their designated slot)
- Chest moves: click slot in inventory, click slot in chest (onMoveStack), cross-container move
- Stack merging: stackable items combine; durability items (tools/weapons/armor) each occupy 1 slot with own durability value
- Slot emptying: count drops to 0 → slot becomes empty
- Overflow handling: full inventory loses dropped item surpluses (no item entity floor drops)
- Insertion order: stackable items fill existing stacks first, then empty slots; durability items take whole empty slots

**Item Tooltip** (`/components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`):
- On hover, displays item name, kind (block/tool/weapon/armor/food/material), and key stats (attack, defense, durability, hunger)

**Armor System:**
- `equippedArmor` record tracks which item ID is in each slot
- Equipped pieces provide defense (5% damage reduction per defense point, capped 75%)
- Breaking unequips automatically; dropping unequips automatically
- All 6 armor types are equippable (not just chestplate)

### 7. KEY PATTERNS FOR ADDING CONTENT

**To add a new block:**
1. Add entry to `BlockId` enum in `/lib/world/blocks.ts`
2. Add color to `BLOCK_COLORS` and `HELD_BLOCK_COLORS`
3. Add hardness to `BREAK_HARDNESS` in `/lib/game/items.ts`
4. Add drop mapping to `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` if it should drop an item
5. If ore: add to worldgen config in `/lib/world/generation.ts` with spawn parameters

**To add a new item:**
1. Add `ItemDef` object to `ITEM_DEFS` array in `/lib/game/items.ts`
2. Set `id`, `label`, `kind` (block/tool/weapon/armor/food/material)
3. If tool: set `minePower`, `mineTier`, `maxDurability`
4. If weapon: set `attack`, optionally `meleeReach`/`throwDamage`, `maxDurability`
5. If armor: set `armorSlot`, `defense`, `maxDurability`
6. If food: set `hunger` value (points restored)

**To add a recipe:**
1. Add `Recipe` object to `RECIPES` array in `/lib/game/recipes.ts`
2. Set `id`, `label` (human-readable formula), `cost` array, `result`
3. Optionally set `station: "furnace"` for smelting-only recipes

This structure makes it straightforward to extend the game content by following the existing patterns for blocks, items, and recipes.

## Thorough Inventory of Mobs, Combat, and Game Systems

### 1. MOBS/ENTITIES

**File Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobs.ts`

**Mob Registry and Data Structure:**
The codebase uses a centralized `MOB_TEMPLATES` record that defines all mob kinds with their stats:

```typescript
export type MobTemplate = {
  speed: number;
  hp: number;
  detectRange: number;
  attackDamage: number;
  attackCooldown: number;
  ranged?: boolean;
  modelArgs: [bodyColor, headColor, legColor, eyeColor, detailColor, bodySize, headSize]
}
```

**Current Mobs (7 total):**

| Mob Kind | HP | Speed | Damage | Detect Range | Type | Drops |
|----------|----|----|--------|--------------|------|-------|
| **sheep** | 10 | 0.9 | 0 (passive) | 0 | Passive/Animal | wool (1-2), raw_mutton (1) |
| **chicken** | 7 | 1.2 | 0 (passive) | 0 | Passive/Animal | feather (0-2), raw_chicken (1) |
| **horse** | 14 | 1.4 | 0 (passive) | 0 | Passive/Animal | leather (1-2) |
| **zombie** | 10 | 1.05 | 3 | 11 | Hostile/Monster | rotten_flesh (1-2) |
| **skeleton** | 9 | 1.08 | 3 | 12 | Hostile/Ranged | bone (1-2) |
| **spider** | 8 | 1.2 | 2 | 10 | Hostile/Monster | string (0-2) |
| **boss** | 400 | 1.1 | 10 (melee) | 28 | Hostile/Boss | dragon_heart (1), diamond_ore (2-4) |

**Mob Data Structure (MobState):**
Located in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/state.ts`:

```typescript
type MobState = {
  id: number;
  kind: MobKind;
  hostile: boolean;
  hp: number;
  position: THREE.Vector3;  // body center
  direction: THREE.Vector3;  // movement direction
  yaw: number;
  turnTimer: number;
  speed: number;
  moveSpeed: number;  // post-aggro speed this tick
  detectRange: number;
  attackDamage: number;
  attackCooldown: number;
  attackTimer: number;
  halfHeight: number;  // collision geometry
  bobSeed: number;  // animation seed
  fedTimer: number;  // breeding: seconds in "love" state
  ageTimer: number;  // breeding: seconds until baby grows up
  summonTimer?: number;  // boss-only: minion summon timer
}
```

- File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobModel.ts`
- Models are procedurally built from box geometries (body, head, 4 legs, eyes, snout, stripe)
- Each mob has color palette + size parameters

### 2. AI/BEHAVIOR

**File Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.ts`

**AI System Overview:**
The `tickMobs()` function (main AI loop, called every frame) handles all mob behavior per-frame tick with dependencies:

```typescript
export type MobTickDeps = {
  surfaceYAt: SurfaceYAtFn;
  applyDamage: (amount: number) => void;
  removeMobAt: (index: number) => void;
  rng: () => number;
  emit: EmitGameEvent;
}
```

**Core Behaviors:**

1. **Wandering (Passive & Hostile)**
   - Default: random direction changes every 1.5-4.5 seconds
   - Mobs turn to face boundaries (world edges at x,z < 2 or > sizeX/Z - 2)
   - Can't path through solid blocks — bounces back and turns

2. **Aggression (Hostile Mobs)**
   - Line-of-sight detection to player within `detectRange`
   - Speed boost when aggro (x1.15 multiplier)
   - Melee reach: 4 blocks (boss: 3.5 blocks)
   - Ranged reach: entire `detectRange` (e.g., skeleton 12 blocks)
   - Vertical gap tolerance for attacking: 1.6 blocks (boss: 3 blocks)

3. **Passive Behavior (Animals)**
   - Flee when player is within 4.2 blocks
   - No aggro, just move away

4. **Spider Special**
   - Hostile only below `SPIDER_AGGRO_BELOW_DAYLIGHT` (0.42 daylight)
   - Passive during daytime (like Minecraft)

5. **Ranged Combat (Skeleton/Boss)**
   - Skeleton: kites within standoff band (min: 5, max: 9 blocks)
   - Boss: approaches (no kiting), melees up close, spreads arrows at range
   - Arrow leading: `SKELETON_LEAD_FACTOR` (0.6) × target velocity × travel time
   - Skeleton arrow speed: 27 m/s (slower than player's 34)
   - Boss fires 3-arrow horizontal spread with angle `BOSS_SPREAD` (0.18 radians)

6. **Melee Combat (Zombie/Spider/Boss)**
   - Attack cooldown per mob (e.g., zombie: 1.35s, spider: 1.1s)
   - On hit: knockback (4.2 units for regular, 6 for boss)
   - Knockback also lifts player vertically (3.4 for regular, 4.5 for boss)

7. **Day/Night Spawning**
   - Zombies & skeletons burn above `HOSTILE_BURN_ABOVE_DAYLIGHT` (0.72 daylight)
   - Damage rate: 0.8 HP/second in full daylight
   - Spider stays passive above threshold

8. **Boss Special (Summoning)**
   - Periodically summons skeleton or zombie minions
   - Summon interval: 12 seconds
   - Minion cap: 4 per boss
   - Respects global `HOSTILE_CAP` (16 total)
   - Only summons while engaged with player and within detect range
   - Summons at random radius 2-4 blocks away

### 3. COMBAT

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/combat.ts` — player attacking
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.ts` — mob attacking
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/projectiles.ts` — arrow system
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/playerLife.ts` — damage & respawn

**Player vs Mob Combat:**

1. **Melee Attack**
   - Function: `tryAttackMob(state, damage, onMobKilled, reach)`
   - Finds nearest mob within reach & aim cone (dot product > 0.89)
   - Default reach: 4.5 blocks (spear: 7 blocks)
   - Base damage: `FIST_DAMAGE` (6) if empty-handed, or weapon.attack value
   - Knockback: 0.75 units horizontal, 0.12 vertical

2. **Ranged Attack (Bow)**
   - Function: `tryFireBow(state, emit)`
   - Requires: bow held, arrow in inventory, cooldown expired
   - Cooldown: 0.4 seconds between shots
   - Arrow damage: 9 (BOW_ARROW_DAMAGE)
   - Arrow knockback: 0.6
   - Durability cost: 1 per shot

**Mob vs Player Combat:**
- Damage calculation: `applyDamageWithArmor(state, amount)` — applies armor reduction
- Armor reduces damage by percentage (varies by equipped armor)
- All damage minimum 1 HP
- Death check: hearts ≤ 0

**Health System:**
- Max health: 20 hearts (MAX_HEARTS)
- Health regeneration at ≥ 12 hunger (REGEN_MIN_HUNGER) every 3 seconds
- Respawn time: 3 seconds (RESPAWN_SECONDS)
- Respawn location: last slept bed, or random land point

### 5. SPAWNING

**File Locations:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/spawnDirector.ts` — spawn mechanics
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/spawn.ts` — surface finding
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts` — spawn config

**Spawn Mechanics:**

1. **Initial Spawn (Day 1)**
   Function: `spawnInitialMobs(state, rng, surfaceYAt)`
   - Around player's spawn location
   - Passives in wider ring (120% of RENDER_RADIUS = ~108 blocks)
   - Hostiles in tighter ring (70% of RENDER_RADIUS = ~63 blocks)
   
   Initial population:
   - 6× sheep (passive)
   - 5× chicken (passive)
   - 3× horse (passive)
   - 8× zombie (hostile)
   - 6× skeleton (hostile)
   - 6× spider (hostile)

2. **Hostile Spawn Director (Night Spawning)**
   Function: `tickHostileSpawnDirector(state, dt, rng, surfaceYAt)`
   - Only spawns when daylight < 0.28 (HOSTILE_SPAWN_BELOW_DAYLIGHT)
   - Interval: 10 seconds (HOSTILE_SPAWN_INTERVAL_SECONDS)
   - Max global cap: 16 hostile mobs (HOSTILE_CAP)
   - Spawn radius: max(26, RENDER_RADIUS × 0.85) = ~76.5 blocks
   - Spawn kinds: randomly picks zombie, skeleton, or spider
   - Count per spawn: 1 or 2 (if RNG > 0.7)

3. **Dungeon Spawner System**
   Function: `tickSpawnerDirector(state, dt, rng, emit)`
   - Drips from block spawners (BlockId.Spawner)
   - Only when player within SPAWNER_ACTIVATION_RADIUS (16 blocks)
   - Spawn interval: 8 seconds (SPAWNER_INTERVAL_SECONDS)
   - Local cap: 6 mobs per spawner (SPAWNER_LOCAL_CAP)
   - Global cap: 16 total hostile (HOSTILE_CAP)
   - Kinds: zombie, skeleton, spider (random)
   - Mobs spawn at spawner block +/- 1.2 blocks XZ jitter

4. **Safe Spawn Validation**
   Function: `findSpawnOnLand(world, centerX, centerZ, seekPlains?)`
   - Requires: solid floor, 2 air blocks above, gentle slope (±2 blocks height diff)
   - Prefers Plains biome, falls back to any biome
   - Uses spiral search up to 300 blocks radius

**File Locations:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/breeding.ts` — animal breeding
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts` — feeding, beds, crafting

- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts` — dungeon loot

**A. Breeding System**

Function: `tickBreeding(state, dt, rng, surfaceYAt, emit)`

- **Feed Items:**
  - Sheep: wheat
  - Horse: wheat
  - Chicken: seeds

- **Mechanics:**
  - Right-click animal with feed item → enters "in love" mode for 30 seconds (BREED_FED_WINDOW_SECONDS)
  - On interval (0.5s), checks for two in-love adults of same kind within 3 blocks radius
  - Spawns baby at midpoint, scaled to 55% size (BABY_SCALE)
  - Baby grows up over 90 seconds (BABY_GROW_SECONDS)
  - No drops when killed as baby
  - Passive population cap: 24 animals (PASSIVE_CAP)
  - Both parents reset love timer after breeding

**B. Boss System**

- **Summon Condition:**
  - Craft "boss_summoner" (diamond-gated cursed totem)
  - Use (right-click) within render range → spawns boss 2-4 blocks away
  - One-time spawn; defeat to win

- **Boss Mechanics:**
  - HP: 400 (insanely durable vs. typical 7-14 mob HP)
  - Melee damage: 10, reach: 3.5 blocks
  - Ranged: 3-arrow spread, damage 7 per arrow, speed 30 m/s
  - Summons minions every 12 seconds (up to 4 while engaged)
  - Drops: dragon_heart (1), diamond_ore (2-4)
  - Dragon heart → dragon_sword craft (best in game, 60 damage)

**C. Loot System**

Mobs drop defined items via `rollMobDrops(kind, rng)`:
- Drops use min/max ranges + optional chance (0..1)
- Example (zombie): 1-2 rotten_flesh (guaranteed)
- Example (spider): 0-2 string (might be 0)
- Dungeon chests: seeded lazy fill on first access (never re-rolls)

**D. Explosions / Special Blocks**

*Not currently implemented* — no TNT or creeper explosions in the codebase.

**E. Taming / Ownership**

*Not implemented* — no taming system for hostile mobs.

---

### 7. HOW TO ADD NEW MOBS

To add a new mob, follow this pattern:

1. **Add to `MobKind` type** (`types.ts`):
   ```typescript
   export type MobKind = "sheep" | ... | "new_mob";
   ```

2. **Define template** (`mobs.ts`):
   ```typescript
   export const MOB_TEMPLATES: Record<MobKind, MobTemplate> = {
     new_mob: {
       speed: 1.0,
       hp: 12,
       detectRange: 10,
       attackDamage: 3,
       attackCooldown: 1.2,
       ranged: false,
       modelArgs: [0xRRGGBB, 0xRRGGBB, 0xRRGGBB, 0xff3333, 0xRRGGBB, [width, height, depth], [head_w, head_h, head_d]]
     }
   };
   ```

3. **Add drops** (`mobLoot.ts`):
   ```typescript
   export const MOB_DROPS: Record<MobKind, MobDrop[]> = {
     new_mob: [
       { itemId: "item_id", min: 1, max: 2 }
     ]
   };
   ```

4. **Add to spawn groups** (`spawnDirector.ts`):
   - Initial spawn: add to groups array in `spawnInitialMobs()`
   - Night spawn: add to `SPAWNER_KINDS` for dungeon spawners
   - Or create custom spawn logic

5. **Optional: Custom AI** (`mobAI.ts`):
   - Add behavior branches in `tickMobs()` for unique mechanics

# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **World types**: when creating a world you now pick a generation preset, not just a seed — **Default**, **Superflat** (level builder's canvas), **Amplified** (towering, dramatic relief), or **Islands** (scattered land in a raised sea). Each generates genuinely different terrain over the same biome map; **zero new assets** (the picker reuses the menu chrome)
  - `generateWorld(world, worldType)` forks on the type before any block write via `terrainConfigFor` (`lib/world/generation.ts`), varying only sea level and per-biome surface height; caves, ores, lava, trees, structures, and dungeons are unchanged. The **Default path is byte-identical** to before — existing worlds and the determinism hashes are untouched, so **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump**. Each new type is pinned by its own SHA-256 baseline plus structural probes (flat is level, amplified spans a wider band, islands floods more of the map while still leaving a dry spawn)
  - The chosen type is stored on `WorldMeta` and in the `SaveData` blob (like `seed`), so a world always regenerates with the type its block-diffs were recorded against. `WorldType` lives in `lib/world/worldTypes.ts`; the schema stays at version 5 (the field is optional, absent ⇒ default)
- **Multiple worlds & player profiles**: the game now opens to a menu instead of dropping straight into one fixed world. A **profile** (name + skin) owns a list of named **worlds**, like user accounts on a shared console — pick a profile, then pick or create a world to play. All client-side in localStorage; **zero new assets** (the menus reuse the existing Minecraft-style chrome and generated skin portraits)
  - **Two-level save hierarchy** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, `lib/game/worlds.ts`): a versioned `minecraft_profiles_v1` manifest (`{ id, name, skinId, createdAt }` + `activeProfileId`) and a `minecraft_worlds_v1` index (`{ id, profileId, name, seed, worldgenVersion, createdAt, lastPlayedAt }`), with each world's `SaveData` in its own `minecraft_world_save_<id>` key. Readers are total — malformed entries are dropped and a dangling active profile is repaired. New-world seeds are resolved once at creation (blank → random, numeric → clamped, other text → stable FNV hash). The skin moved off the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key onto the profile; **audio settings stay global**
  - **Menu shell** (`components/GameShell.tsx`, `components/menu/*`): a profile-select → world-select → play state machine with create / rename / delete on both levels (deleting a profile cascades its worlds and their save blobs). Switching worlds, Load, or Reset remounts the game subtree via a React `key`, so the engine/renderer are disposed and re-created **without a page reload**. The tab remembers its active world in `sessionStorage`, so a reload resumes straight back into play while a fresh tab cold-starts at the menu. The pause menu gains **Save & Quit to Worlds**
  - **Per-world worldgen versioning**: each world records the `WORLDGEN_VERSION` it was generated under (`lib/game/config.ts`). A future worldgen change bumps that constant and discards only the affected worlds' stale block-diffs (rebooting them from their seed) — replacing the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` rename, which reset every world. The save _schema_ is unchanged (still version 5)
  - **First run & migration** (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`): a brand-new player is greeted by the create-profile form (name + skin) — their first act is making a profile. An existing single-world save is instead folded into a default "Player" profile (carrying the old skin) plus one "My World"; the legacy blob is copied before the old key is removed, so a failure can't lose data

## [0.8.0] - 2026-06-14

### Changed

- Starting play now requires a double-click to acquire pointer lock, preventing an accidental single click from immediately capturing the mouse. No save-format or worldgen impact.
- Emergency unstuck now requires **`Shift` + `U`** instead of a bare `U`, so a stray keypress can no longer teleport the player mid-play. The automatic unstuck safeguard (which fires when wedged in terrain or falling below the world) is unchanged. No save-format or worldgen impact.
- Placed glass now renders as a clear, low-reflection transparent layer. Internal faces between adjacent glass blocks are culled, while neighboring opaque block faces remain visible through it. No save-format or worldgen impact.

### Added

- **Caves & lighting**: caves are no longer lit like the surface — a real per-voxel light system makes underground dark, with torches, deep-cave lava, and drowning as new hazards. All procedural, **zero new assets**
  - **Per-voxel light propagation** (`lib/world/lighting.ts`): each voxel carries packed sky + block light (0–15 each), baked from the blocks at load (top-down sunlight column pass + flood) and patched locally on every edit with a Minecraft-style remove/refill flood. It is a **derived cache, never serialized** (a pure function of `world.blocks`). The mesher emits a per-vertex `aLight` attribute and the world material's shader gates scene-lit terrain by sky exposure (caves go dark) while adding block light back as a glow — so **day/night needs no re-mesh** (the scene sun/hemisphere already scale with daylight)
  - **Torches**: craft four from one wood and place them to light the dark; a torch is a solid block emitting block light 14, so its warm glow floods the surrounding air and survives the night
  - **Lava**: pools in the deepest caves (carved air at or below `lavaLevel`), glowing and emitting max block light. Touching it burns **immediately** for 3 hearts every half-second with no grace period, bypassing armor, and keeps burning briefly after you escape. Worldgen-only — no item, can't be mined
  - **Drowning**: a bubble meter (above the hunger bar) drains over ~15 s while your head is underwater, then deals 1 heart/second until you surface; it refills quickly. Distinct from the existing 60 s body-immersion damage — wading chest-deep never drowns you
  - **Worldgen re-baseline**: the deep-cave lava changes the deterministic terrain baseline, so the worldgen/meshing hashes were re-rolled and **`SAVE_KEY` bumped `minecraft_save_v6` → `minecraft_save_v7`** — existing worlds reset. The save _schema_ is unchanged (lighting is derived, lava is worldgen; neither is persisted), so the payload version stays 5. `placeLava` runs after `placeOres` and before `placeDungeons`, keeping the surface byte-identical (structural probes stay green)
- **Spears and non-stackable gear**: seven craftable spear tiers (wood, stone,
  sliver, ruby, sapphire, gold, diamond) have 7-block left-click melee reach and
  can be thrown with right-click/`E`. Throws use procedural projectile meshes,
  gravity, swept mob collision, terrain collision, a cooldown, tiered damage,
  and one durability per throw. Missed throws remain visibly embedded in terrain
  for two seconds, and the faster, flatter trajectory travels farther. All
  durable armor, tools, knives, swords, and spears now occupy one slot across
  drops, crafting, saves, and chests; legacy stacked durable gear is split
  during migration. No save-format or worldgen impact.
- **Wood doors**: craft one from 6 planks and place it on a solid floor to create a thin, two-block-tall panel. Right-click either half to open or close the whole door; open doors rotate onto their hinge edge, while closed doors block players and mobs. Door facing, open state, and both halves persist through the existing block-diff save, and breaking either half removes the whole door and returns one item. Only the player interaction path toggles doors; mobs stop at closed doors and cannot operate them. No save-format or worldgen impact.
- **Water building and exposure damage**: block placement may now replace water cells, so underwater structures can be built against submerged terrain. Remaining continuously immersed for more than 60 seconds starts dealing 3 HP (1.5 hearts) of armor-bypassing damage every second; leaving water fully resets both timers. Exposure is session-only and is not serialized. No save-format or worldgen impact.
- **Endgame & ranged combat**: the game gains its first ranged weapon, ranged enemies, and a true win condition — all on one new transient projectile system, with **no save-format or worldgen change** and **zero new assets** (procedural pixel sprites + synthesized sounds)
  - **Projectile system** (`lib/game/engine/projectiles.ts`, `engine/systems/projectileAI.ts`): arrows are session-only entities (like mobs, never serialized) that integrate under gravity and despawn on a hit, a TTL, or leaving the world. Block collision reuses the world DDA (`voxelRaycast`), which already sweeps the whole segment, so even a very fast arrow can't tunnel a 1-block wall; the entity test is substepped too so a fast arrow can't skip a thin mob between frames. A `fromPlayer` flag is the hit filter — player arrows hit mobs (with knockback), mob arrows hit the player (armor-mitigated) — so a mob's arrow can never hit the firer or another mob. `tickProjectiles` runs right after `tickMobs` in both the live and dead step branches
  - **Bow & arrows**: craft a **bow** (3 wood + 3 string) and **arrows** (1 stone + 1 wood + 1 feather → 4). Holding a bow makes the attack input fire instead of melee — **instant click-to-fire** (no draw-charge; holding left-click already means mine) at a fixed damage on a short cooldown, spending one arrow and a point of bow durability per shot. The swing animation still plays
  - **Ranged skeletons**: a `ranged` flag on the mob template makes skeletons **kite** — backing off inside a standoff band, holding in the middle, approaching only when far — and loose arrows from across their detect range (leading a moving target) instead of meleeing. Finally distinct from zombies, which stay melee
  - **Endgame boss & victory**: craft a **Cursed Totem** (1 diamond ore + 2 bone + 2 gold ore — the diamond grind's first real goal) and right-click to summon a towering boss nearby (refused if one already walks). It bears down on the player, bites for heavy damage up close, looses a 3-arrow spread at range, periodically conjures minions (capped, under the global hostile cap), and is immune to the daylight burn. Defeating it drops a **Dragon Heart** that crafts the best-in-game **Dragon Sword** (60 attack), fires a one-shot **victory screen**, and shows a top-center **boss health bar** while it lives
  - New `"boss"` `MobKind` touches all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables (templates, drops, ambience intervals, ambient + attack sounds). New events (`arrowHit`, `bowFired`, `bossSummoned`, `bossDefeated`, `summonFailed`) drive procedural impact sparks, a conjuring column, a victory burst, and synthesized bow-twang / arrow-tick / boss-roar / victory-fanfare sounds. In-flight arrows render as a pooled extruded-sprite mesh (one shared geometry/material) oriented along their velocity
  - **No version bump**: the boss, its minions, projectiles, and the victory flag are all transient; the new items (bow, arrow, Cursed Totem, Dragon Heart, Dragon Sword) are additive, saved by string id. New tunables live in the `config.ts` "Ranged combat & endgame" group

## [0.7.0] - 2026-06-14

### Added

- **Dungeons & loot**: digging underground now turns up small cobblestone rooms (speckled with mossy cobble) sealed in the rock, well clear of spawn — the game's first real exploration payoff, and a found-loot purpose for chests. All procedural, **zero new assets**
  - Each dungeon holds 1–2 **pre-filled loot chests** and a central **mob spawner**. Loot is tiered (`lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts`): common chests carry food, low ores, and the occasional stone pickaxe (bone always drops, so a chest is never empty); a 25% rare roll adds the payoff — diamond/sapphire ore, a ruby/sapphire sword, helmet/chestplate, or a rare diamond pickaxe
  - **Spawners** drip a hostile (zombie/skeleton/spider) onto the room floor every ~8 s while you're within ~16 blocks, up to 6 clustered nearby (under the global hostile cap). They're time-independent (dungeons are dark); **mining the spawner block out** stops it for good (it's hard and drops nothing). New `mobSpawned` event drives a synthesized low whoosh and a dark conjuring smoke puff
  - New **MossyCobblestone** (mineable into a `mossy_cobble` item, found-only) and **Spawner** blocks, both with procedurally-painted atlas tiles

> AGENT

I've now got a complete picture of the game's current content. Here's my honest assessment before I ask you which direction to plan for.

## What you already have (more than you might think)

This game is **not** content-starved. The inventory:

- **48 blocks**, **62 items**, **48 recipes**
- **7 pickaxe tiers**, **8 sword tiers**, **7 throwable spear tiers**, a **bow**, **6 armor pieces**
- **5 ore tiers** (sliver → ruby → gold → sapphire → diamond)
- **7 mobs** including a **summonable boss** with a victory screen
- Farming, breeding, dungeons + spawners, chests, furnace smelting, a per-voxel lighting system, doors, beds

## Mapping your specific suggestions

| Your idea | Status | My take |
|---|---|---|
| **Coal** | ❌ Missing | **Worth it.** The furnace economy is thin — it "fakes" fuel by consuming planks as a recipe ingredient (no real fuel slot). Coal would add a genuine early-game resource and a reason to mine shallow. |
| **Iron Ore** | ✅ Already exists as **Sliver Ore** | Sliver *is* your iron — the tier-2, stone-pickaxe-mineable ore. Adding literal iron would just duplicate it and confuse the progression. Skip. |
| **Creepers** | ❌ Missing (no explosions at all) | **Top pick.** This is the single biggest "Minecraft feel" gap. |
| **Villagers** | ❌ Missing (no trading/villages) | Great, but the **largest** scope — its own arc. |
| **Crafting Tables** | ⚠️ N/A by design | Crafting here is *always* available via the recipe book; there's no table gate. Adding one is a *restriction*, not fun. The furnace already proves the "station-gated recipe" pattern if you want stations. I'd skip gating. |

## What I actually recommend

**You don't need more swords or ore tiers — you have a ton.** The fun is in **new mechanics and creature variety**, not another tier. Ranked by fun-per-effort:

1. **Explosions — Creeper + TNT** (my #1). A whole new threat *and* a demolition tool, off one explosion system. The block-edit → re-light → re-mesh infra already exists, so the plumbing is friendly.
2. **Coal + a real fuel economy.** Shallow coal ore, coal-as-fuel, charcoal from smelting logs, torches gated on coal. Fixes the thinnest part of the game.
3. **Cow + Pig.** Near-free — just mob templates, drops, and spawn entries. Adds beef/porkchop/leather variety. Great quick win.
4. **Villagers + trading.** The big economy arc; best saved for its own dedicated effort.

Other strong candidates if none of these grab you: **fishing** (self-contained, gives water a purpose).

Let me pin down what to actually write a plan for:

# Phased Implementation Plan: Cow/Pig, Coal, Creeper/TNT, Villagers

## Foundational facts I confirmed by reading the code (these shape the designs)

1. **`blockChanges.set()` is the single chokepoint** (`lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts:22`). Every tracked write does three things atomically: writes the voxel, calls `applyEdit(world, world.light, x, y, z)` to **incrementally relight**, and updates the delta map. It does **not** set `worldMeshDirty` — callers set that separately.

2. **`worldMeshDirty` is a boolean, and remesh is a fixed-cost full-region rebuild** (`GameRenderer.ts:450-472`). `syncWorldMesh` rebuilds the entire `RENDER_RADIUS` region geometry whenever the flag is set, regardless of how many blocks changed. **Critical perf consequence: destroying 40 blocks costs 40 cheap localized `applyEdit` relights + exactly ONE remesh** — the same remesh you already pay for a single mined block. Mass destruction is therefore cheap as long as it routes through `blockChanges.set` and sets `worldMeshDirty = true` once. No new perf path is needed.

3. **The furnace is a pure UI gate, not a machine** (`interact.ts:133-138`, `GameEngine.ts:286-297`, `InventoryPanel.tsx:153-175`). `interactFurnace` just sets `state.craftingStation = "furnace"`. Smelting recipes are ordinary `RECIPES` entries with `station:"furnace"`; the recipe book unlocks them when that station is open, and crafting consumes the listed ingredients (currently `planks`) instantly. There is **no fuel slot, no burn timer, no block-entity state on the furnace**. This decisively favors Phase 2 option (b).

4. **Mob death + loot is centralized** in `GameEngine.removeMobAt` (`GameEngine.ts:501-518`): splice, `rollMobDrops`, add to inventory, emit `mobDied` + `pickedUp`. Drops go straight to inventory (no ground items).

5. **Damage to player** flows through `this.applyDamage` (armor-aware, `applyDamageWithArmor`) and `this.applyEnvironmentalDamage` (unmitigated). Both are already passed into `mobTickDeps`.

6. **Transient-entity pattern** (`projectiles.ts` + `state.ts` `ProjectileState`): a typed array on `GameState`, a `nextXId` counter, a tick system, never serialized. This is the template for TNT primed entities and the explosion-particle event.

7. **Worldgen determinism**: `generation.test.ts` pins `world.blocks` SHA-256 (`:72`) plus a meshing snapshot (`:343`). Any new ore/structure in `generateWorld` changes these hashes. The modern re-baseline mechanism is **`WORLDGEN_VERSION`** (`config.ts:174`, currently `7`) recorded per-world in `worlds.ts` — bumping it discards only affected worlds' block-diffs. The old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` rename is legacy. **Save schema stays version 5** for all four phases (everything new is additive items/blocks via the block-diff, or transient).

8. **Every `Record<MobKind>` table** that must get an entry per new mob: `MOB_TEMPLATES` (`mobs.ts:15`), `MOB_DROPS` (`mobLoot.ts:20`), `CALL_INTERVALS` (`mobAmbience.ts:7`), `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS` (`soundParams.ts:441`), `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS` (`soundParams.ts:564`), and the `MobKind` union (`types.ts:52`). These are exhaustive `Record`s, so TypeScript strict will fail compilation until each is filled — a useful forcing function.

---

## Phase 1 — Cow + Pig (template follow, keep concise)

Two passive mobs cloning the sheep/chicken pattern. New raw+cooked meat food items, leather generalized off horse.

### File-by-file
- **`lib/game/types.ts:52`** — add `"cow" | "pig"` to `MobKind`.
- **`lib/game/mobs.ts`** — two `MOB_TEMPLATES` entries. Cow: reuse horse-ish dimensions, white/brown palette, `hp:10, speed:0.9, detectRange:0, attackDamage:0`. Pig: smaller, pink palette, `hp:8, speed:1.0`. `modelArgs` is the 7-tuple `[bodyColor, headColor, legColor, eyeColor, detailColor, bodySize[3], headSize[3]]` — no new model code, `createMobModel` already parameterizes everything.
- **`lib/game/mobLoot.ts:20`** — `cow: [{leather 1-2}, {raw_beef 1-1}]`, `pig: [{raw_porkchop 1-1}]`.
- **`lib/game/items.ts:96-107`** — add food items: `raw_beef` (hunger 3), `cooked_beef` (hunger 8), `raw_porkchop` (hunger 3), `cooked_porkchop` (hunger 8). `leather` already exists (line 94).
- **`lib/game/recipes.ts`** — two furnace recipes following `cook_chicken` exactly: `cook_beef` (`raw_beef + planks → cooked_beef`, `station:"furnace"`), `cook_porkchop` likewise. **Note:** if Phase 2 lands, retrofit these to coal fuel; if Phase 1 ships first, use `planks` to match the existing convention.
- **`interact.ts:106` `FEED_ITEMS`** — `cow: "wheat"`, `pig: "seeds"` (carrots don't exist; seeds fits and is already a pig analog feed). This is `Partial<Record<MobKind>>` so it's not exhaustive, but add both.
- **`spawnDirector.ts:82` `spawnInitialMobs`** — add `["cow", false, 4, passiveRadius]`, `["pig", false, 4, passiveRadius]`. Passive ongoing spawns: there is currently **no passive spawn director** (only `tickHostileSpawnDirector` and the spawner director). If "ongoing passive spawns" is required, that's a small new function — **confirm with human** whether passives should repopulate or stay initial-only (current sheep/chicken/horse are initial-only + breeding). Recommend: rely on breeding (already wired) rather than add a passive director, to match existing behavior.
- **Exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables** (compiler-forced): `mobAmbience.ts:7` `CALL_INTERVALS`, `soundParams.ts:441` `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS`, `soundParams.ts:564` `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS` (passives have attackDamage 0 but the table is still exhaustive — give them a benign idle sound, mirroring how sheep/chicken are populated). Procedural moo/oink sound params (zzfx tuples).

### Save / worldgen
None. Mobs and breeding state are transient. New items persist by string id in existing slots. No `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump.

### Tests / docs
- Extend `mobLoot.test.ts` (it enforces drop item-ids exist in `ITEM_DEFS`), `breeding.test.ts` (cow/pig love→baby), a recipe test for the two cook recipes.
- Docs: `adding-content.md` (already the template), `reference.md`, `manual.md`. No save-format/tuning change.

### Risk
Lowest. Only watch the exhaustive-Record compile errors (they're the checklist).

## Phase 2 — Coal & fuel economy

### Recommendation: **Option (b), the lightweight smelting-ingredient + torch-gate.** 

Justification grounded in the code: the furnace has **zero block-entity state** — it's a UI flag (`craftingStation`). Option (a) would require inventing a furnace block-entity (a `Map<voxelIndex, {fuel, input, output, burnTime}>` like `containers`), a per-tick smelting system, **new save-format state** (the furnace store would have to serialize like chests → schema change or a parallel `containers`-style map), a redesigned furnace panel with input/fuel/output slots, and progress UI. That is a multi-day arc for a fuel meter. Option (b) reuses the existing recipe machinery verbatim and ships in hours. The "fakes fuel with planks" design is already established and accepted; coal simply becomes a better fuel ingredient. **Best fun/effort/risk ratio by a wide margin.**

### Design
- **New `CoalOre` block** (`blocks.ts` enum, next free id 48). Tier-1 / stone-pickaxe-mineable: confirmed mapping in `mining.ts:24-32` — `canMineBlock` returns `toolTier >= 1` only for Stone/Cobble/Brick; **everything not explicitly listed returns `true` (minable by hand)**. To gate coal behind a stone pickaxe like Minecraft, add `if (block === BlockId.CoalOre) return toolTier >= 1;` to `canMineBlock`. (Tier 1 = wood pickaxe per `items.ts:82`; if you want "stone pickaxe" specifically that's tier 2 — **confirm with human**: Minecraft coal needs only a wooden pickaxe = tier 1. Recommend tier 1.)
- **Block tables**: `BLOCK_COLORS`, `HELD_BLOCK_COLORS`, `BREAK_HARDNESS` (≈6, between stone and sliver), `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` (`CoalOre → "coal"`), atlas tile in `atlas.ts` (dark speckled tile — the `drawTile` switch). Add to `mining.ts canMineBlock`.
- **New items** (`items.ts`): `coal` (material), `charcoal` (material, interchangeable fuel).
- **Worldgen** (`generation.ts` `GEN.oreConfigs:44`): add `{ id: BlockId.CoalOre, attempts: ~160000, minY: 4, maxYOffset: 6, minSize: 4, maxSize: 12 }` — shallow (high `maxYOffset` keeps `y` low? note the math: `y = minY + rand*(sizeY - maxYOffset)`, so a *small* `maxYOffset` → larger Y range → can spawn high; a *large* `maxYOffset` → shallower. To make coal shallow+common, use a small `maxYOffset` so it spans up toward the surface, with high attempts). **Place CoalOre first in the array** (before sliver) so it's the most common. Re-derive exact params against the determinism test.
- **Recipes** (`recipes.ts`):
  - Smelt logs → charcoal: `{ id:"charcoal", cost:[{wood 1}], result:{charcoal 1}, station:"furnace" }`.
  - Retarget cook recipes from `planks` fuel to `coal`/`charcoal`. Since a recipe's `cost` is a fixed ingredient list (no "OR" support in the `Recipe` type), the simplest path is **two recipe variants per cook** (one with coal, one with charcoal) OR keep a single recipe using `coal` only and let charcoal be the logs-derived equivalent crafted into coal — **confirm with human**. Recommend: add `cook_*_coal` and `cook_*_charcoal` variants; trivial duplication, no type change.
  - Torch gate: change `torch` recipe (`recipes.ts:28`) from `1 wood → 4 torch` to `1 coal + 1 wood → 4 torch` (Minecraft-style). This is the only existing-recipe edit; flag it as a **balance change a human should confirm** (it makes early torches require finding coal).

### Save / worldgen
- **New ore in `generateWorld` ⇒ determinism re-baseline.** Per the lava/caves/dungeon precedent (CHANGELOG lines 33, 62): re-roll the `generation.test.ts` block-hash (`:72`), the typed-world hashes, and the meshing snapshot (`:343`); **bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION` 7 → 8** (`config.ts:174`). Worlds on v7 get their block-diffs discarded and reboot from seed. **Save schema stays v5** (coal/charcoal are additive items, CoalOre persists via block-diff like any block). Structural probes (surface untouched, since ore is underground) should stay green — keep coal below the surface band.

### Tests / docs
- `generation.test.ts`: update the pinned digests; add a probe that CoalOre appears at shallow depth and is more common than sliver.
- Recipe tests for charcoal + the retargeted cook/torch recipes.
- `mining.test.ts` (if present) / a `canMineBlock` unit test for the coal tier gate.
- Docs: `save-format.md` (add a v5/WORLDGEN_VERSION-8 bullet mirroring the lava entry), `tuning.md` (ore params + torch recipe change), `adding-content.md`, `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Sequencing / risk
- Phase 2 is **independent of Phase 1** but both touch `recipes.ts` cook entries — land Phase 1 first, then Phase 2 retargets its cook recipes to coal. If reversed, Phase 1's cook_beef/cook_porkchop should be authored with coal directly.
- The torch-recipe change is the only player-facing balance risk; everything else is additive.

---

## Phase 3 — Creeper + TNT (the novel explosion system) — DESIGN DEPTH

This is the core new mechanic. The architecture splits cleanly into **one reusable explosion primitive** consumed by **two triggers** (creeper detonation, TNT fuse).

### 3a. The explosion primitive — new `lib/game/engine/systems/explosion.ts`

**Signature:**
```
export function explode(state, cx, cy, cz, opts: { power: number }, deps: ExplosionDeps): void
```
where `ExplosionDeps = { applyDamage(amount): void; surfaceYAt; rng; emit }` — the same shape already passed around as `mobTickDeps`, so the engine wires it with its existing closures.

**What it does, in order:**

1. **Block destruction.** Iterate a cube `[-R..R]³` around the center (`R = ceil(power)`, e.g. power 3 → radius 3, ~123 cells but only those within the sphere). For each cell within `dist <= power`:
   - Skip protected blocks: `Bedrock`, `Spawner`, `Lava`, and any block not in `BLOCK_TO_SLOT`/with `BREAK_HARDNESS` ≥ a blast-resistance threshold. Concretely, introduce a small `blastResistance(block)` helper (a `Partial<Record<BlockId, number>>` defaulting to a low value; Bedrock/Spawner = Infinity, Stone/Cobble/Brick = high-but-finite, dirt/sand/wood/leaves = low). Destroy when `power * (1 - dist/power) > blastResistance(block)` — Minecraft-style distance falloff. Keep it simple: **a single threshold pass**, not full ray-traced blast rays (that's gold-plating for a voxel toy).
   - For each destroyed cell call `state.blockChanges.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Air)`. **This is the load-bearing reuse**: each call relights locally via `applyEdit` and records the delta. Optionally drop a fraction of loot (`rollBlockDrops` gated by a low chance) — **recommend NO loot drop from explosions** (matches Minecraft TNT mostly destroying, and avoids inventory spam since drops auto-collect). Confirm with human.
   - **Set `state.worldMeshDirty = true` exactly once** after the loop — the single remesh covers all destroyed blocks (per Foundational Fact #2). **Do not set it inside the loop.**

2. **Door special-case:** if a destroyed cell is a door half, mirror `mining.ts:122-128` to remove the paired half. Reuse that logic (consider extracting a tiny `removeBlockAt` helper shared by mining and explosion, but not required).

3. **Player damage with falloff.** Compute `dist = player.position.distanceTo(center)`; if `dist < power * 2`, `damage = round(maxDamage * (1 - dist/(power*2)))`. Call `deps.applyDamage(damage)` (armor-aware via `applyDamageWithArmor`). Apply **knockback**: push `player.velocity` away from center, scaled by `(1 - dist/range)` (mirrors the melee knockback in `mobAI.ts:209-214`).

4. **Mob damage with falloff.** Loop `state.mobs`; for each within range, subtract falloff damage from `mob.hp`, apply knockback to `mob.position`/`mob.direction` (mirror `combat.ts:76-82`), and collect dead indices. **Removal subtlety:** the engine's `removeMobAt` splices by index and drops loot — call it for dead mobs **after** the loop, descending index order (the established pattern in `mobAI.ts:235`). This means explosion damage naturally **chains** (a creeper blast can kill other creepers → which detonate next tick — fine, even fun). Pass `deps.removeMobAt` in.

5. **Emit an event** `{ type: "explosion", x, y, z, power }` (new `GameEvent` variant in `state.ts:263`). The renderer (`GameRenderer.ts:203` event switch) emits a large procedural particle burst (reuse `particles.emitBurst` with smoke/fire colors + bigger count scaled by power), and `audioDirector.ts:184` plays a new synthesized `EXPLOSION_SOUND` (zzfx boom tuple in `soundParams.ts`). Both are **zero-asset**, consistent with the core principle.

**Where it lives & what it touches:**
- New file `systems/explosion.ts`. Touches: `blockChanges` (mass `.set`), `worldMeshDirty` (once), `applyDamage`/`removeMobAt` (via deps), `state.mobs`, and emits one event. It does **not** need to touch `mining.ts`, `playerLife.ts`, or `lighting.ts` directly — it reuses them through the existing chokepoint. This is the key design property: **the explosion is just a batched mining operation + an AoE damage loop**, riding rails that already exist.

### 3b. Creeper mob

- **`types.ts`** `MobKind += "creeper"`.
- **New `MobState` fields** (`state.ts:26`): `fuseTimer: number` (counts down once primed; `0`/unset = not primed). Since `MobState` is transient (never serialized), this is free — no save impact. Add `fuseTimer: 0` to `pushMob` (`spawnDirector.ts:31`).
- **`mobs.ts` `MOB_TEMPLATES`**: creeper entry — `hostile`, green palette, `hp:10, speed:1.0, detectRange:11, attackDamage:0` (it doesn't bite — it explodes), `attackCooldown` unused. Mark with a template flag, e.g. `explodes: true` and `fuseSeconds: 1.5`, `fuseRange: 2.5`, `explosionPower: 3` (add these optional fields to `MobTemplate`).
- **`mobAI.ts` `tickMobs`** — add a creeper branch. Creeper uses the **existing hostile chase** (it already lerps toward the player when `activeHostile && dist < detectRange`). The novel bit:
  - When `attackDistance < fuseRange` and not yet primed → start fuse: `mob.fuseTimer = fuseSeconds`, emit `{ type:"mobAttacked", kind:"creeper" }` (drives the hiss sound), and the renderer swells the model (see 3d).
  - While primed: `mob.fuseTimer -= dt`. If the player walks out of a larger "abort range," reset `fuseTimer = 0` (Minecraft creepers abort) — **confirm with human** whether to allow abort or commit. Recommend allow-abort (more fun, less cheap).
  - When `fuseTimer <= 0`: call `explode(state, mob.position.x, mob.position.y, mob.position.z, {power: explosionPower}, deps)` then **mark the creeper dead** (push its index to `deadIndices`). It dies in its own blast and drops via `removeMobAt` (gunpowder).
  - **Killing before detonation:** already handled — `combat.ts tryAttackMob` and arrows reduce `mob.hp`; at `hp<=0` `removeMobAt` fires, dropping gunpowder. A creeper killed mid-fuse simply never reaches `fuseTimer<=0`. No special code.
  - **`mobAI.ts` needs `explode` + `removeMobAt` in its deps.** `removeMobAt` is already in `MobTickDeps`. Add `explode` to `MobTickDeps` (or call a thin `deps.explode`). Wire in `GameEngine.mobTickDeps` (`:540`).
- **`mobLoot.ts`**: `creeper: [{ itemId:"gunpowder", min:1, max:2 }]`.
- **Spawn**: night/light-gated like zombie. Add `"creeper"` to `SPAWNER_KINDS`/`tickHostileSpawnDirector` spawn list (`spawnDirector.ts:104`) and optionally `spawnInitialMobs`. Creeper should burn in daylight like zombies (`mobAI.ts:228` condition) — add it to that clause (`kind !== "spider" && kind !== "boss"` already excludes the right ones; creeper is included by default, good).
- **All exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables**: `MOB_TEMPLATES`, `MOB_DROPS`, `CALL_INTERVALS`, `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS`, `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS` (the hiss is the "attack" sound). 

### 3c. TNT block + gunpowder + ignition

- **New items** (`items.ts`): `gunpowder` (material, creeper drop), `tnt` (block, `blockId: BlockId.Tnt`).
- **New block** `Tnt` (`blocks.ts` enum next id): `BLOCK_COLORS`/`HELD_BLOCK_COLORS` (red+white striped), `BREAK_HARDNESS` (low, ~1), `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` (`Tnt → "tnt"`), atlas tile. TNT is a normal placeable/minable block until ignited.
- **Recipe** (`recipes.ts`): `{ id:"tnt", cost:[{gunpowder 4},{sand 1}], result:{tnt 1} }` (Minecraft-flavored; tune counts).
- **Ignition trigger — recommendation: right-click TNT with a Torch** (the existing held item, `BlockId.Torch` / item id `"torch"`). This avoids inventing a flint&steel item (no new item, no new recipe) and slots cleanly into the existing right-click precedence in `interact.ts tryInteractBlock`. Add `Tnt` to `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` only when the held item is a torch — but `tryInteractBlock` keys off the *aimed block*, not the held item. Cleaner: handle ignition in `tryUseHeldItem` (`interact.ts:164`) — add a branch: if held item is `"torch"` and aimed block is `Tnt`, prime it. **Confirm with human**: torch-ignite vs. a new flint&steel item. Recommend torch (zero new content).
- **Priming model — reuse the transient-entity pattern.** TNT, once ignited, becomes a **primed-TNT entity** rather than staying a block (so it can have a fuse and, optionally, fall/launch). Two options:
  - **(Simplest) In-place block fuse:** keep a `Map<voxelIndex, fuseTimer>` of primed TNT on `GameState` (session-only, like a lightweight container map), tick it down in a new `tickPrimedTnt` system, and on expiry replace the block with Air (`blockChanges.set`) and call `explode(...)` at the block center. No new entity type, no renderer model change (the block just sits there glowing/flashing via a render tint until it blows). **Recommend this** for effort.
  - (Fuller) A `PrimedTntState[]` transient entity with a model that flashes and can be launched by nearby blasts (chaining). More work, more juice. Defer unless desired.
  - Either way: **TNT chains** because `explode` destroys neighboring TNT blocks — to make chained TNT *also* detonate (not just vanish), the destruction loop should special-case `Tnt`: instead of setting Air, prime it with a short random fuse. That's the satisfying Minecraft behavior and is a 3-line special-case in `explosion.ts`. **Confirm with human** whether chaining is wanted (recommend yes — it's the fun).
- **State**: add `primedTnt: Map<number, number>` (+ no `nextId` needed since keyed by voxel index) to `GameState` and `createTimers`/constructor init. **Transient, never serialized** — matches mobs/arrows.
- **Tick wiring** (`GameEngine.step`): call `tickPrimedTnt(state, dt, explodeDeps)` alongside the other systems (e.g. after `tickMobs`).

### 3d. Renderer / audio (zero-asset)

- **`GameRenderer.ts:203` event switch**: handle `explosion` → big smoke/fire `emitBurst` scaled by `power`.
- **Creeper fuse swell** (`mobVisuals.ts`): when `mob.fuseTimer > 0`, scale the creeper model up and flash white (a uniform scale + emissive pulse driven off `fuseTimer`). This is presentation-only off the transient `fuseTimer` field.
- **`audioDirector.ts:184`**: `explosion` → new `EXPLOSION_SOUND`; creeper `mobAttacked` → hiss in `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS.creeper`.
- **`soundParams.ts`**: new `EXPLOSION_SOUND` zzfx tuple, creeper ambient + hiss tuples.

### Save / worldgen
- **No worldgen change** (creeper is spawned, TNT is crafted — like the boss). **No `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump.**
- **No save-schema change (stays v5).** TNT *blocks* persist via the block-diff like any block. Gunpowder/tnt items persist by string id. **Primed TNT fuse state and creeper fuse state are transient** (never serialized) — exactly the boss/arrow precedent (CHANGELOG line 32). Document this explicitly in `save-format.md`: "primed TNT and creeper fuse are session-only; a save mid-fuse loads with the TNT as an un-primed block / no creeper."

### Tests
- **`explosion.test.ts`** (new, headless — the engine already runs headless per `GameEngine.test.ts`): assert a blast at a known center clears the expected sphere, leaves Bedrock/Spawner intact, respects blast-resistance threshold, damages a planted player/mob with correct falloff, applies knockback, emits one `explosion` event, and sets `worldMeshDirty` exactly once. Inject `rng` for determinism.
- **Creeper AI** in `mobAI.test.ts`: fuse starts in range, counts down, detonates at 0, aborts when player leaves (if abort chosen), dies in own blast, drops gunpowder.
- **TNT**: ignition via torch primes; `tickPrimedTnt` detonates at fuse end; chaining primes neighbor TNT.
- **Loot/recipe**: `mobLoot.test.ts` (gunpowder exists), recipe test for tnt.
- No `generation.test.ts` change (no worldgen).

### Docs
`adding-content.md` (document the explosion primitive as the new reusable system + how to give any feature a blast), `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `tuning.md` (power/fuse/blast-resistance constants → all in `config.ts`), `save-format.md` (transient-fuse note), `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Risks / human-confirm
1. **Loot from blasted blocks** (recommend none).
2. **Creeper fuse abort** vs commit (recommend abort).
3. **TNT ignition**: torch right-click vs new flint&steel (recommend torch).
4. **TNT chaining** (recommend yes — special-case `Tnt` in the destruction loop).
5. **Primed-TNT as block-map vs entity** (recommend block-map for v1).
6. **Blast through unmineable**: confirm Spawner/Bedrock/Lava are protected (yes in design).
7. Perf sanity: with `power 3` the worst case is a few hundred cell tests and a few dozen `applyEdit` relights — well within a frame. If a human wants bigger blasts, the only cost that scales badly is many simultaneous large blasts in one tick; cap concurrent detonations if needed.

---

## Phase 4 — Villagers + trading (the big arc) — DESIGN DEPTH, sub-phased

Sequence as **4a structures → 4b villager mob → 4c trading UI/economy**, each independently shippable.

### 4a. Village structures in worldgen

**Reuse `placeStructures`/`placeHouse` (`generation.ts:400-449`), not `placeDungeons`.** The existing `placeHouse` already builds above-ground brick houses on validated flat ground using `highestSolidY`. A "village" is a **cluster of houses placed near a common center**, optionally with a well/lamp-post.

- Add `villageCount` to `GEN` and a `placeVillages(world, rand)` that, per village, picks a center, then places N houses in a ring (reusing `placeHouse` or a `placeVillageHouse` variant with a door + interior). Call it in `generateWorld` after `placeStructures`, before `placeDungeons`.
- **Determinism subtlety (critical):** villagers must spawn at village sites on load. Following the **dungeon precedent** (`generation.ts:451-561`), villager spawn points must be **re-derivable from the seed without reading blocks**. Add a `collectVillageSites(world, worldType)` that replays the village placement math against a **dedicated seed-only PRNG** (`dungeonRand`-style) and **seed-pure `terrainTopY`** (not `highestSolidY`), mirroring `collectDungeonSites`. Store the resulting spawn anchors in a session-only set on `GameState` (like `dungeonSpawnerIndices`). This is the established, proven pattern — copy it.
- **Worldgen change ⇒ determinism re-baseline + `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump** (8 → 9 if Phase 2 landed, else 7 → 8). Re-roll `generation.test.ts` digests + meshing snapshot. Save schema stays v5. Structural probes: villages are on the surface, so the surface DOES change — update/relax the surface-untouched probe or add a "villages exist near expected centers" probe. **This is the one phase that visibly changes the surface baseline** — call it out loudly.

### 4b. Villager mob

- **`types.ts`** `MobKind += "villager"`.
- **`mobs.ts`**: villager template — **passive but does NOT flee**. The current passive AI (`mobAI.ts:151`) makes passives flee within 4.2m. Add a per-template flag `wandersNeutral: true` (or check `kind === "villager"`) so the villager skips the flee branch and only does the idle wander (`mobAI.ts:154`). `hp:20, speed:0.6, attackDamage:0`.
- **`mobLoot.ts`**: villager drops nothing (or minor). Still needs a `MOB_DROPS.villager` entry (exhaustive) — `[]` is fine.
- **Spawn**: villagers spawn at village anchors on load (new `spawnVillageMobs(state, ...)` called from the constructor like `spawnInitialMobs`, iterating the `collectVillageSites` anchors), capped per village. They're persistent residents, not director-trickled. **Confirm with human**: respawn villagers if killed? Recommend no (they're transient; reload respawns them from seed anchors, which is acceptable and matches dungeon-mob semantics).
- **All exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables**: templates, drops, `CALL_INTERVALS`, `MOB_AMBIENT_SOUNDS` (a "hmm" mumble), `MOB_ATTACK_SOUNDS` (benign, attackDamage 0).

### 4c. Trading economy + UI

**Currency decision — recommendation: `emerald` as a trade-only item, NOT a new ore.** Adding an emerald ore would force *another* worldgen re-baseline and another `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump stacked on 4a. Making emerald a **trade-obtained currency** (you sell materials to villagers FOR emeralds, then buy goods WITH emeralds) keeps the economy self-contained, avoids extra worldgen churn, and mirrors Minecraft villager trading. **Confirm with human**: emerald-as-ore (mineable, more worldgen) vs emerald-as-trade-item (recommended).

- **New item** `emerald` (material) in `items.ts`.
- **Trade table** — new data module `lib/game/trades.ts`: a `TRADES: TradeOffer[]` array, `TradeOffer = { id, give: {slotId,count}[], get: {slotId,count}, maxUses }`. Buy offers (`give: emerald → get: tool/food/block`) and sell offers (`give: material → get: emerald`). This is the trading analog of `RECIPES`.
- **Trade UI — model on the chest/inventory panel.** The cleanest reuse: trading is essentially "a recipe book filtered to trade offers, opened by a station." 
  - **Interact**: in `interact.ts`, add `tryTradeAimedVillager` to the right-click precedence in `GameEngine.dispatch` `placeBlock` (`:328`) — right-clicking a villager (via `findAimedMobIndex`) opens trade mode. Set a new `state.tradingVillagerId` (session) and `state.inventoryOpen = true`, plus a new `craftingStation`-like flag, OR generalize `craftingStation` to `"furnace" | "trade"`. Recommend extending the station union to `"furnace" | "villager"` so the existing recipe-book UI machinery (`InventoryPanel.tsx:153`, `GameSnapshot.craftingStation`) is reused with minimal new surface.
  - **New `TradePanel.tsx`** (or a trade section inside `InventoryPanel`) modeled on the `recipe-book` block: list `TRADES`, each row shows give-icons → get-icon, click to execute via a new `trade` command (parallel to `craft`). Reuse `ItemIcon`, `createSlot`, the disabled/`canCraft` pattern.
  - **`commands.ts`**: new `{ type:"trade", offerId }`. **`GameEngine.dispatch`**: a `trade` case mirroring `craft` (validate the villager is open, check ingredients via `inv.countsById`, deduct gives, add get, decrement that offer's remaining uses).
- **Restock mechanic**: per-offer `usesRemaining` that refills on a timer. Since villagers are transient, keep restock **session-only** on the `MobState` (add an optional `tradeUses?: Record<string, number>` to the villager's `MobState`, or a parallel session map keyed by mob id). Tick a `breedTimer`-style restock timer to refill. **Confirm with human**: do trade uses need to persist across reload? Recommend **no** — keep it transient/seeded like dungeon loot (reload = fresh stock), which sidesteps any save-format change. This is the explicitly-suggested "keep transient/seeded like dungeon loot" approach.

### Save / worldgen (Phase 4)
- **4a structures ⇒ worldgen re-baseline + `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump** (and the surface baseline genuinely changes — distinct from all prior underground-only re-baselines). Re-roll `generation.test.ts`.
- **4b/4c ⇒ no save-schema change (stays v5).** Villagers are transient (re-spawned from seed-derived village anchors on load, dungeon-mob precedent). Emerald + traded items persist by string id in existing slots. Trade-use/restock state is session-only. **No schema bump** if you accept transient restock (recommended). If a human insists trade progress must persist, that becomes a v6 schema change (a `villagerTrades` block-entity-like map) — a meaningfully bigger task; flag it.

### Tests
- `generation.test.ts`: new digests + a "villages near expected centers" structural probe + a `collectVillageSites` determinism test (same anchors derived twice, and anchors match what `placeVillages` wrote — the dungeon-test pattern).
- `trades.test.ts`: offer validation (item ids exist in `ITEM_DEFS`), trade execution deducts/adds correctly, restock refills.
- Villager AI test: wanders, does NOT flee the player.
- A component test for `TradePanel` (the repo has component tests).

### Docs
`adding-content.md` (document the village-structure + seed-derived-anchor pattern and the trade system as the recipe analog), `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `tuning.md` (village counts, trade table, restock interval), `save-format.md` (villages = worldgen re-baseline + `WORLDGEN_VERSION` note; villagers/trades transient), `CHANGELOG.md`.

### Risks / sequencing / human-confirm
1. **Emerald: ore vs trade-item** (recommend trade-item — avoids a second worldgen bump).
2. **Trade-use persistence** (recommend transient — avoids schema change; otherwise v6).
3. **Surface determinism baseline changes** — this is the first surface-altering re-baseline; the structural probes that currently assert "surface untouched" must be updated, not just the hashes. Highest-attention item in Phase 4.
4. **Villager flee exemption** must be added carefully to `mobAI.ts` so it doesn't regress sheep/cow/pig flee behavior — gate strictly on the villager flag.
5. **Sequencing**: 4a must land before 4b (villagers need anchors). 4c depends on 4b. Within the four phases, **Phase 4 should be last** (biggest, and its worldgen bump is cleanest applied once after Phase 2's coal bump rather than interleaved).
6. **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` bumps stack**: Phase 2 (coal) bumps once, Phase 4a (villages) bumps again. Each discards affected worlds' diffs. Batch them if shipping together to avoid two consecutive world resets.

## Cross-cutting summary

| Phase | New `MobKind` | New blocks | Worldgen change? | `WORLDGEN_VERSION` | Save schema |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Cow/Pig | cow, pig | none | no | no bump | v5 (unchanged) |
| 2 Coal | none | CoalOre, (Tnt is P3) | **yes (new ore)** | **bump** | v5 (additive items) |
| 3 Creeper/TNT | creeper | Tnt | no (spawned/crafted) | no bump | v5 (transient fuse) |
| 4 Villagers | villager | none (emerald=item) | **yes (villages, surface)** | **bump** | v5 (transient trades) |

The single most important architectural realization for the parent agent: **the explosion system (Phase 3) is not a new subsystem — it is a batched loop over the existing `blockChanges.set` chokepoint plus the existing `applyDamage`/`removeMobAt` deps, finished with one `worldMeshDirty = true`.** Everything else is template-follow. Design effort should concentrate on (a) the `explode()` falloff/blast-resistance/chaining rules and (b) Phase 4's seed-derived village anchors + the surface determinism re-baseline.

- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts (coal ore + village structures + the `collectDungeonSites` seed-derivation pattern to copy — Phases 2 & 4)
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts (tick wiring, `removeMobAt` loot, `mobTickDeps`, dispatch precedence for ignite/trade — all phases)
- /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts (cook/charcoal/torch/tnt recipes — Phases 1, 2, 3) and the new /Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/explosion.ts (the novel primitive — Phase 3)

Creeper mob\n- `types.ts` `MobKind += \"creeper\"`; `MobState` += transient `fuseTimer` (free — never serialized); init in `pushMob`.\n- `mobs.ts` — green, `hostile`, `hp:10, speed:1.0, detectRange:11, attackDamage:0`; optional template fields `explodes:true, fuseSeconds:1.5, fuseRange:2.5, explosionPower:3`.\n- `mobAI.ts` — reuse existing hostile chase; when within `fuseRange` start fuse (emit hiss via `mobAttacked`); tick down; on `<=0` call `explode(...)` and mark creeper dead (dies in its blast, drops gunpowder via `removeMobAt`). Killing mid-fuse needs no special code (hp→0 path already removes + drops). Add `explode` to `MobTickDeps`, wire in `GameEngine.mobTickDeps`. Include creeper in the daylight-burn clause + night spawn lists.\n- `mobLoot.ts` — `creeper: [gunpowder 1-2]`. Fill exhaustive sound/ambience tables (hiss as attack sound).\n\n### 3c. TNT + gunpowder\n- `items.ts` — `gunpowder` (material), `tnt` (block item). `blocks.ts` — `Tnt` block (red/white striped tile, low hardness, normal placeable/minable until ignited).\n- `recipes.ts` — `tnt`: `4 gunpowder + 1 sand → 1 tnt`.\n- **Ignition:** in `interact.ts` `tryUseHeldItem`, if held item is `\"torch\"` and aimed block is `Tnt`, prime it (zero new item — avoids inventing flint&steel).\n- **Priming (recommended v1):** session-only `primedTnt: Map<voxelIndex, fuseTimer>` on `GameState` (transient like containers); new `tickPrimedTnt` system ticks down, on expiry sets the block to Air + calls `explode` at block center. **Chaining:** the destruction loop special-cases `Tnt` — instead of Air, prime it with a short random fuse.\n- Wire `tickPrimedTnt` into `GameEngine.step` after `tickMobs`.\n\n### 3d. Presentation (zero-asset)\n- `mobVisuals.ts` — creeper swells + flashes white while `fuseTimer > 0`.\n- `GameRenderer.ts` event switch — `explosion` → big smoke/fire burst.\n- `audioDirector.ts` / `soundParams.ts` — `EXPLOSION_SOUND` boom, creeper hiss + ambient zzfx tuples.\n\n**Save/worldgen:** **none.** Creeper spawned, TNT crafted (boss precedent). Primed-TNT + creeper fuse are transient. Schema v5; document in `save-format.md` that a save mid-fuse loads with TNT un-primed / no creeper.\n\n**Tests/docs:** new `explosion.test.ts` (headless: clears expected sphere, Bedrock/Spawner intact, falloff damage+knockback, one event, `worldMeshDirty` set once); creeper AI in `mobAI.test.ts`; TNT ignite/detonate/chain; loot+recipe tests. Docs: `adding-content.md` (document the explosion primitive as a reusable system), `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `tuning.md` (power/fuse/blast-resistance constants in `config.ts`), `save-format.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n**Decisions (defaults chosen):** no loot from blasted blocks; creeper fuse **aborts** if player flees; **torch** ignition; TNT **chains**; primed-TNT as block-map (not entity) for v1.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 4 — Villagers + trading (the big arc; sub-phase 4a→4b→4c)\n\n**Goal:** give exploration a social/economy purpose. Ships last (biggest, and its worldgen bump is cleanest applied once).\n\n### 4a. Village structures\n- Reuse `placeHouse`/`placeStructures` (`generation.ts`), **not** dungeons — houses already build above-ground on validated flat ground. Add `placeVillages(world, rand)` placing N houses in a ring around a center; call after `placeStructures`, before `placeDungeons`.\n- **Determinism:** villagers must spawn at village sites on load *without reading blocks*. Copy the **dungeon precedent** — add `collectVillageSites(world, worldType)` replaying placement math against a seed-only PRNG + seed-pure `terrainTopY` (mirror `collectDungeonSites`), store anchors in a session-only set on `GameState`.\n- **Worldgen change ⇒ re-baseline + `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump** (8 → 9). **This is the first surface-altering re-baseline** — update the \"surface untouched\" structural probes, not just the hashes. Schema stays v5.\n\n### 4b. Villager mob\n- `types.ts` `MobKind += \"villager\"`; `mobs.ts` — passive but **does NOT flee** (add `wandersNeutral` flag; `mobAI.ts` skips the flee branch for it — gate strictly so sheep/cow/pig flee unchanged). `hp:20, speed:0.6, attackDamage:0`.\n- `mobLoot.ts` — `villager: []`. Spawn at village anchors on load via new `spawnVillageMobs` (constructor, like `spawnInitialMobs`), capped per village. Fill exhaustive sound tables (mumble).\n\n### 4c. Trading economy + UI\n- **Currency = `emerald` as a trade-only item** (recommended) — avoids a *second* worldgen bump that an emerald ore would force. New `emerald` material in `items.ts`.\n- New `lib/game/trades.ts` — `TRADES: TradeOffer[]` (`{id, give[], get, maxUses}`); buy offers (emerald → goods) + sell offers (materials → emerald). The trade analog of `RECIPES`.\n- **UI:** extend the `craftingStation` union to `\"furnace\" | \"villager\"` so the existing recipe-book panel machinery is reused. Right-click a villager (via `findAimedMobIndex` in `GameEngine.dispatch`) sets `tradingVillagerId` + opens inventory in trade mode. New `TradePanel` section modeled on the recipe-book block (reuse `ItemIcon`, `createSlot`, `canCraft`-style disabled state). New `{type:\"trade\", offerId}` command + a `dispatch` case mirroring `craft` (deduct gives, add get, decrement uses).\n- **Restock:** session-only `tradeUses` on the villager `MobState`, refilled on a timer — transient/seeded like dungeon loot (reload = fresh stock), so **no save-format change**.\n\n**Save/worldgen:** 4a bumps `WORLDGEN_VERSION` (surface baseline changes); 4b/4c transient (villagers respawn from seed anchors; emerald/items persist by string id; trade state session-only). Schema stays v5.\n\n**Tests/docs:** `generation.test.ts` (new digests + \"villages near expected centers\" probe + `collectVillageSites` determinism test); `trades.test.ts`; villager-no-flee AI test; `TradePanel` component test. Docs: `adding-content.md` (village seed-anchor pattern + trade system), `reference.md`, `manual.md`, `tuning.md`, `save-format.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n**Decisions (defaults chosen):** emerald = **trade-item** not ore; trade-use state **transient** (no schema bump — persisting it would be a v6 change); villagers **respawn from seed anchors** (no persistence).\n\n---\n\n## Cross-cutting summary\n\n| Phase | New MobKind | New blocks | Worldgen change | WORLDGEN_VERSION | Save schema |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 Cow/Pig | cow, pig | — | no | no bump | v5 |\n| 2 Coal | — | CoalOre | **yes (new ore)** | **bump 7→8** | v5 |\n| 3 Creeper/TNT | creeper | Tnt | no | no bump | v5 |\n| 4 Villagers | villager | — (emerald=item) | **yes (villages, surface)** | **bump 8→9** | v5 |\n\n**Sequencing:** 1 → 2 → 3 → 4. Phase 1 before 2 (Phase 2 retargets Phase 1's cook recipes to coal). Phase 4 last (4a before 4b before 4c). **Two `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bumps stack** (Phase 2 coal, Phase 4a villages) — each discards affected worlds' diffs; batch if shipped together to avoid two consecutive world resets. Each phase is its own focused branch + PR per AGENTS.md.\n\n## Verification (per phase, before each PR)\n\n- Gate (CI `verify`): `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build` all green. `bun run test:e2e` for Phase 3 (renderer/explosion particles) and Phase 4 (trade UI).\n- The exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` compile errors are the per-mob checklist — `bun run typecheck` won't pass until every table has the new entry.\n- Phases 2 & 4a change worldgen determinism hashes — re-baseline `generation.test.ts` deliberately (confirm the diff is only the intended new ore/structures), and verify surface structural probes (untouched for coal, updated for villages).\n- Manual gameplay pass (Phase 3 especially): craft TNT, ignite with a torch, confirm crater + chained detonation + damage falloff; let a creeper approach, confirm fuse hiss/swell, detonation, and that killing it mid-fuse drops gunpowder and cancels the blast. Phase 4: find a village, right-click a villager, complete a buy and a sell trade, confirm restock.\n- Confirm docs + `CHANGELOG.md` updated for every touched tunable/system/mechanic/save-impact (part of the gate, not an afterthought).\n"
}

> AGENT

The plan is written. Here's the bottom line on your question, then the plan for approval.

**My honest take:** the game is already content-rich (48 blocks, 62 items, 48 recipes, 7 mobs + a boss, 8 sword tiers, 7 pickaxe tiers, farming, breeding, dungeons, lighting). It doesn't need *more* swords or ore tiers — those have diminishing returns. The fun is in **new mechanics and creatures**. On your specific list: **Iron Ore already exists as Sliver Ore** (don't duplicate it), and **Crafting Tables don't fit** — crafting here is intentionally always-on via the recipe book, so a table gate would just be a restriction. **Coal, Creepers, and Villagers are the real, worthwhile gaps**, and you picked all four to plan.

The plan phases them by fun-per-effort:

1. **Cow + Pig** — near-free template-follow (beef/porkchop/leather variety). No save/worldgen impact.
2. **Coal & fuel economy** — shallow coal ore, charcoal, coal-gated torches. Recommends the lightweight "coal as smelting ingredient" approach over a full fuel-slot rebuild (the furnace has zero block-entity state today). Triggers a worldgen re-baseline + `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump.
3. **Creeper + TNT** — the novel core. The key insight: the explosion system is *not* a new subsystem, just a batched loop over the existing block-edit chokepoint (auto-relights) + existing damage deps, with one remesh. No save/worldgen impact.
4. **Villagers + trading** — the big arc; reuses the dungeon's seed-derived-anchor pattern for villages, emerald as a trade-only currency (avoids a second ore re-baseline), trade UI reusing the recipe-book panel.

A few player-facing decisions have recommended defaults baked in (coal-gated torches, torch-ignited TNT, TNT chaining, emerald-as-item) — all noted in the plan and easy to flip.

> AGENT

My honest take: **one branch + one PR works here** — these four phases are a cohesive "content expansion" arc, which is exactly the "one reviewable unit" AGENTS.md wants a feature branch for. Two refinements I'd strongly recommend:

1. **One focused commit per phase** (Conventional Commits, with a body explaining the why), keeping `bun run lint` green at each commit so the branch stays bisectable. That gives you the reviewability benefit of slices without multiple PRs.
2. **The two `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bumps** (Phase 2 coal, Phase 4a villages) collapse into **one** when shipped together — bump to the final version once at the end so worlds reset a single time, not twice.

My one caveat: this is a large PR (4 features, 2 of them touching worldgen determinism). It'll be a heavy review. If at any point you'd rather merge incrementally, the per-phase commits make it trivial to split later. But as a single arc on one branch, it's reasonable.
