  "old_string": "- The renderer auto-renders any arrow via `projectileVisuals.ts` (a pooled extruded `arrow` sprite); add an impact event (e.g. `arrowHit`) for particles/sound if you want feedback.\n\n## A new player skin preset",
  "new_string": "- The renderer auto-renders any arrow via `projectileVisuals.ts` (a pooled extruded `arrow` sprite); add an impact event (e.g. `arrowHit`) for particles/sound if you want feedback.\n\n## An explosion (creeper, TNT, or anything that blows up)\n\n- The blast itself is one reusable primitive: `explode(state, cx, cy, cz, power, deps)` in `lib/game/engine/systems/explosion.ts`. It destroys blocks in a `power`-radius sphere (distance falloff vs each block's `blastResistance`, skipping the unbreakable set), then damages the player and mobs out to **twice** that radius with the same falloff, applies knockback, and emits one `explosion` event. `deps` is `{ applyDamage, rng, emit }` — a subset of `MobTickDeps`, so a mob can pass its own `deps` straight in.\n- It is **not** a new subsystem: it batches over the existing `blockChanges.set` chokepoint (each removed cell relights locally and records the save delta) and sets `worldMeshDirty` **once**, so a whole crater costs a single remesh — the same one a single mined block pays. It only lowers mob hp (never splices), so it is safe to call mid `tickMobs`; the caller sweeps any mob at 0 hp.\n- To give a new thing a blast, just call `explode(...)`. The **creeper** lights a fuse near the player then detonates (`mobAI.ts`, `MobState.fuseTimer`); **TNT** is a placeable block lit by right-clicking it with a torch (`primeTnt` in `interact.tryUseHeldItem`), counted down and detonated by `tickPrimedTnt` (a `state.primedTnt` map, session-only). Both are transient — nothing here is serialized.\n- Tunables (power, fuses, peak damage, chain delay) live in `config.ts`; the per-block `blastResistance` and unbreakable set live in `explosion.ts`. Cover new blasts in `explosion.test.ts` (headless: assert the crater, spared unbreakables, falloff, one remesh/event).\n\n## A new player skin preset"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/CHANGELOG.md",
  "old_string": "### Added\n\n- **Coal & a fuel economy**: a new shallow ore deepens the early game",
  "new_string": "### Added\n\n- **Creepers, TNT & explosions**: the game gains its first explosion system — a sneaking mob that detonates and a craftable demolition charge — all on **zero new assets** (procedural model/atlas + synthesized boom)\n  - **Explosion primitive** (`lib/game/engine/systems/explosion.ts`): `explode()` destroys blocks in a power-radius sphere (distance falloff vs each block's blast resistance, sparing bedrock/spawner/lava), then damages the player and mobs out to twice that radius with the same falloff, applies knockback, and emits one `explosion` event. It is **not a new subsystem** — it batches over the existing `blockChanges.set` chokepoint (each cell relights locally, records the save delta) and sets `worldMeshDirty` **once**, so a whole crater costs a single remesh. It only lowers mob hp (never splices), so it is safe to run mid mob-tick; the caller sweeps the dead\n  - **Creeper**: a hostile mob that chases silently, then lights a ~1.5 s fuse within ~2.6 blocks (hissing and swelling white), and **explodes** — dying in its own blast and dropping **gunpowder**. Walk past it while primed and the fuse aborts; kill it first to take the gunpowder safely. Burns in daylight like a zombie\n  - **TNT**: craft it (4 gunpowder + 1 sand), place it like a block, and **right-click it with a torch** to light a fuse (the torch isn't consumed). It detonates after a delay and **chains** adjacent TNT. Lit fuses are session-only (`state.primedTnt`); a save written mid-fuse reloads with the TNT inert\n  - **Minimum hostile spawn distance** (`HOSTILE_SPAWN_MIN_RADIUS`, 16): every hostile spawn (initial + night trickle) now keeps a standoff from the player, so nothing — least of all a creeper — can materialize point-blank\n  - The new `\"creeper\"` `MobKind` fills all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables. Explosions drive a procedural fireball + smoke burst and a synthesized boom; a primed creeper swells and flashes via its `MobState.fuseTimer`. **No save-format or worldgen change**: creepers are spawned, TNT is crafted, fuses are transient, and the new items save by string id\n- **Coal & a fuel economy**: a new shallow ore deepens the early game"
}
```

  "command": "git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfeat(combat): add creepers, TNT, and an explosion system\n\nThe game's first explosions: a mob that detonates and a craftable demolition\ncharge, both built on one reusable primitive.\n\n- explosion.ts `explode()` destroys blocks in a power-radius sphere (distance\n  falloff vs each block's blast resistance, sparing bedrock/spawner/lava), then\n  damages the player and mobs out to twice that radius with falloff + knockback,\n  emitting one event. It is not a new subsystem: it batches over the existing\n  blockChanges.set chokepoint (each cell relights + records the save delta) and\n  sets worldMeshDirty once, so a whole crater costs a single remesh. It only\n  lowers mob hp (never splices), so it is safe to call mid mob-tick; tickMobs now\n  sweeps the dead after the loop, which also collects blast kills.\n- Creeper: a hostile that chases silently, lights a ~1.5s fuse within range\n  (hissing, swelling, flashing), then explodes — dying in its blast and dropping\n  gunpowder. Walking away aborts the fuse; killing it first cancels it.\n- TNT: craft from gunpowder + sand, place it, right-click with a torch to light\n  the fuse (torch not consumed), and it detonates and chains adjacent TNT. Lit\n  fuses (creeper fuseTimer, primedTnt map) are session-only.\n- Hostiles now keep a 16-block minimum spawn distance (HOSTILE_SPAWN_MIN_RADIUS)\n  so nothing — least of all a creeper — materializes point-blank.\n\nProcedural only (model/atlas tile + synthesized boom), so no new assets, and no\nsave-format or worldgen change (creepers spawn, TNT crafts, fuses are transient).\nAdds explosion/creeper/TNT-ignition tests; docs and CHANGELOG updated.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -4",
  "description": "Commit Phase 3"
}

398	function placeTrees(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
399	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.treeAttempts; i += 1) {
400	    const x = 4 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 8));
401	    const z = 4 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 8));
402	
403	    const biome = world.getBiome(x, z);
404	    const spawnChance = GEN.biomes[biome].treeChance;
405	
406	    if (rand() > spawnChance) continue;
407	
408	    const topY = world.highestSolidY(x, z);
409	    if (world.get(x, topY, z) !== BlockId.Grass) continue;
410	
411	    const trunkHeight = spawnChance > 0.5 ? 4 + Math.floor(rand() * 3) : 3 + Math.floor(rand() * 3);
412	    for (let y = 1; y <= trunkHeight; y += 1) world.set(x, topY + y, z, BlockId.Wood);
413	
414	    const leafStart = topY + trunkHeight - 1;
415	    for (let ox = -2; ox <= 2; ox += 1) {
416	      for (let oz = -2; oz <= 2; oz += 1) {
417	        for (let oy = 0; oy <= 2; oy += 1) {
418	          const d = Math.abs(ox) + Math.abs(oz) + oy;
419	          if (d > 4) continue;
420	          // Only fill air: the canopy must not eat the trunk or neighbors.
421	          if (world.get(x + ox, leafStart + oy, z + oz) !== BlockId.Air) continue;
422	          world.set(x + ox, leafStart + oy, z + oz, BlockId.Leaves);
423	        }
424	      }
425	    }
426	  }
427	}
428	
429	function placeCacti(world: VoxelWorld): void {
430	  // Hash-gated scan instead of random attempts: density follows the dry
431	  // desert area, so a mostly-flooded desert gets few cacti instead of the
432	  // whole budget missing. Doesn't consume the shared rand stream.
433	  for (let x = 4; x < world.sizeX - 4; x += 1) {
434	    for (let z = 4; z < world.sizeZ - 4; z += 1) {
435	      if (hash2D(x * 1.7 + 11.3, z * 2.3 - 7.1) > GEN.cactusChance) continue;
436	      if (world.getBiome(x, z) !== BiomeId.Desert) continue;
437	
438	      const topY = world.highestSolidY(x, z);
439	      if (world.get(x, topY, z) !== BlockId.Sand) continue;
440	      if (world.get(x, topY + 1, z) !== BlockId.Air) continue;
441	
442	      const height = 2 + (hash2D(z * 3.1, x * 1.3) > 0.5 ? 1 : 0);
443	      for (let y = 1; y <= height; y += 1) world.set(x, topY + y, z, BlockId.Cactus);
444	    }
445	  }
446	}
447	
448	function placeStructures(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
449	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.houseCount; i += 1) {
450	    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
451	    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
452	    placeHouse(world, cx, cz);
453	  }
454	}
455	
456	function placeHouse(world: VoxelWorld, cx: number, cz: number): void {
457	  const half = 3;
458	  const floorY = world.highestSolidY(cx, cz) + 1;
459	  if (floorY < 3 || floorY + 6 >= world.sizeY - 1) return;
460	
461	  for (let x = cx - half - 1; x <= cx + half + 1; x += 1) {
462	    for (let z = cz - half - 1; z <= cz + half + 1; z += 1) {
463	      if (!world.inBounds(x, floorY - 1, z)) return;
464	      const y = world.highestSolidY(x, z);
465	      if (Math.abs(y - (floorY - 1)) > 1) return;
466	    }
467	  }
468	
469	  for (let x = cx - half; x <= cx + half; x += 1) {
470	    for (let z = cz - half; z <= cz + half; z += 1) world.set(x, floorY - 1, z, BlockId.Cobblestone);
471	  }
472	
473	  for (let y = floorY; y <= floorY + 3; y += 1) {
474	    for (let x = cx - half; x <= cx + half; x += 1) {
475	      for (let z = cz - half; z <= cz + half; z += 1) {
476	        const wall = x === cx - half || x === cx + half || z === cz - half || z === cz + half;
477	        if (!wall) continue;
478	        const isWindow = y === floorY + 1 && ((x === cx && (z === cz - half || z === cz + half)) || (z === cz && (x === cx - half || x === cx + half)));
479	        world.set(x, y, z, isWindow ? BlockId.Glass : BlockId.Brick);
480	      }
481	    }
482	  }
483	
484	  world.set(cx, floorY + 1, cz - half, BlockId.Air);
485	  world.set(cx, floorY + 2, cz - half, BlockId.Air);
486	
487	  for (let y = floorY; y <= floorY + 3; y += 1) {
488	    world.set(cx - half, y, cz - half, BlockId.Wood);
489	    world.set(cx + half, y, cz - half, BlockId.Wood);
490	    world.set(cx - half, y, cz + half, BlockId.Wood);
491	    world.set(cx + half, y, cz + half, BlockId.Wood);
492	  }
493	
494	  for (let x = cx - half - 1; x <= cx + half + 1; x += 1) {
495	    for (let z = cz - half - 1; z <= cz + half + 1; z += 1) world.set(x, floorY + 4, z, BlockId.Planks);
496	  }
497	}
498	
499	// ── Dungeons ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
500	// Small underground cobblestone rooms with pre-fillable loot chests and a mob
501	// spawner. The chest CONTENTS are not generated here (the block-diff save can't
502	// carry worldgen-placed entities), only the blocks — the engine fills chests
503	// lazily on first open. To support that, the engine re-derives the chest and
504	// spawner voxel indices on load via collectDungeonSites(), which replays this
505	// exact placement math. Two invariants keep build and derive in lockstep:
506	//   1. A dedicated PRNG seeded only from world.seed (independent of the shared
507	//      generation stream), and every dungeon draws a fixed number of values up
508	//      front so the stream stays aligned whether or not the dungeon is placed.
509	//   2. Placement validates against terrainTopY() — a seed-pure surface estimate
510	//      — never highestSolidY(), which a cave or a player edit could shift.
511	
512	type DungeonSink = { chest: (idx: number) => void; spawner: (idx: number) => void };
513	
514	const NOOP_DUNGEON_SINK: DungeonSink = { chest: () => {}, spawner: () => {} };
515	
516	/** A PRNG seeded only from the world seed, decoupled from the main gen stream. */
517	function dungeonRand(seed: number): () => number {
518	  let t = (seed ^ 0x9e3779b9) >>> 0;
519	  return () => {
520	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
521	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
522	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
523	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
524	  };
```

524	  };
525	}
526	
527	/**
528	 * The generation-time terrain surface at (x,z), computed purely from the seed
529	 * (mirrors the topY math in generateTerrain). Unlike highestSolidY it reads no
530	 * blocks, so it is identical at generation and at load — the property that lets
531	 * collectDungeonSites reproduce the exact dungeon layout.
532	 */
533	function terrainTopY(world: VoxelWorld, x: number, z: number, cfg: TerrainConfig): number {
534	  const biome = world.getBiome(x, z);
535	  const noise = smoothNoise2D(x, z, world.seed);
536	  const { baseHeight, noiseScale } = cfg.biomes[biome];
537	  return Math.max(5, Math.min(world.sizeY - 5, Math.floor(baseHeight + noise * noiseScale)));
538	}
539	
540	function buildDungeonRoom(world: VoxelWorld, cx: number, cz: number, floorY: number, secondChest: boolean, write: boolean, sink: DungeonSink): void {
541	  const half = 3; // 7x7 outer, 5x5x4 hollow interior
542	  if (write) {
543	    for (let y = floorY - 1; y <= floorY + 4; y += 1) {
544	      for (let x = cx - half; x <= cx + half; x += 1) {
545	        for (let z = cz - half; z <= cz + half; z += 1) {
546	          if (!world.inBounds(x, y, z)) continue;
547	          const interior = x > cx - half && x < cx + half && z > cz - half && z < cz + half && y >= floorY && y <= floorY + 3;
548	          if (interior) {
549	            world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Air);
550	          } else {
551	            const mossy = hash2D(x * 1.7 + y * 0.9, z * 2.3 - y * 0.6) > 0.6;
552	            world.set(x, y, z, mossy ? BlockId.MossyCobblestone : BlockId.Cobblestone);
553	          }
554	        }
555	      }
556	    }
557	    world.set(cx - 2, floorY, cz - 2, BlockId.Chest);
558	    if (secondChest) world.set(cx + 2, floorY, cz + 2, BlockId.Chest);
559	    world.set(cx, floorY, cz, BlockId.Spawner);
560	  }
561	  sink.chest(world.index(cx - 2, floorY, cz - 2));
562	  if (secondChest) sink.chest(world.index(cx + 2, floorY, cz + 2));
563	  sink.spawner(world.index(cx, floorY, cz));
564	}
565	
566	function buildDungeons(world: VoxelWorld, write: boolean, sink: DungeonSink, cfg: TerrainConfig): void {
567	  const rand = dungeonRand(world.seed);
568	  const centerX = world.sizeX / 2;
569	  const centerZ = world.sizeZ / 2;
570	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.dungeonCount; i += 1) {
571	    // Draw every random up front so the stream advances identically whether or
572	    // not this dungeon turns out to be placeable.
573	    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
574	    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
575	    const floorRoll = rand();
576	    const secondChest = rand() < 0.6;
577	
578	    const surface = terrainTopY(world, cx, cz, cfg);
579	    const minFloor = 7;
580	    const maxFloor = surface - 7; // keep the ceiling >=3 below the surface
581	    if (maxFloor < minFloor) continue;
582	    // Keep dungeons clear of the spawn area so there's no free starter loot.
583	    if (Math.hypot(cx - centerX, cz - centerZ) < 40) continue;
584	
585	    const floorY = minFloor + Math.floor(floorRoll * (maxFloor - minFloor + 1));
586	    buildDungeonRoom(world, cx, cz, floorY, secondChest, write, sink);
587	  }
588	}
589	
590	function placeDungeons(world: VoxelWorld, cfg: TerrainConfig): void {
591	  buildDungeons(world, true, NOOP_DUNGEON_SINK, cfg);
592	}
593	
594	export type DungeonSites = { chestIndices: number[]; spawnerIndices: number[] };
595	
596	/**
597	 * Re-derives the voxel indices of every dungeon chest and spawner WITHOUT
598	 * writing blocks, by replaying buildDungeons' placement math. The engine calls
599	 * this once after regenerating the world on load to rebuild the session-only
600	 * dungeon index sets that gate lazy loot fill and spawner activation. It must be
601	 * passed the same worldType the world was generated with — dungeon depths follow
602	 * the type's terrain, so the derived indices match what placeDungeons wrote.
603	 */
604	export function collectDungeonSites(world: VoxelWorld, worldType: WorldType = "default"): DungeonSites {
605	  const chestIndices: number[] = [];
606	  const spawnerIndices: number[] = [];
607	  buildDungeons(world, false, { chest: (idx) => chestIndices.push(idx), spawner: (idx) => spawnerIndices.push(idx) }, terrainConfigFor(worldType));
608	  return { chestIndices, spawnerIndices };
609	}
610

  "content": "import type { Recipe } from \"@/lib/game/types\";\n\n/**\n * Villager trade offers. A trade is just a station-gated recipe (`station:\n * \"villager\"`), so it flows through the same crafting machinery and recipe-book\n * UI as everything else — it is only craftable while a villager trade is open.\n *\n * The economy is two-sided: SELL offers turn gathered materials into emeralds\n * (the currency), BUY offers spend emeralds on goods. There is no per-trade use\n * cap yet — trading is bounded by what you can gather, which keeps it self-\n * limiting without any persisted villager state.\n */\nexport const TRADES: Recipe[] = [\n  // ── Sell (materials → emeralds) ──\n  { id: \"trade_wheat\", label: \"6 Wheat -> 1 Emerald\", cost: [{ slotId: \"wheat\", count: 6 }], result: { slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_coal\", label: \"3 Coal -> 1 Emerald\", cost: [{ slotId: \"coal\", count: 3 }], result: { slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_leather\", label: \"2 Leather -> 1 Emerald\", cost: [{ slotId: \"leather\", count: 2 }], result: { slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_gold\", label: \"1 Gold Ore -> 1 Emerald\", cost: [{ slotId: \"gold_ore\", count: 1 }], result: { slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  // ── Buy (emeralds → goods) ──\n  { id: \"trade_bread\", label: \"1 Emerald -> 2 Bread\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }], result: { slotId: \"bread\", count: 2 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_torch\", label: \"1 Emerald -> 8 Torch\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 1 }], result: { slotId: \"torch\", count: 8 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_arrows\", label: \"2 Emerald -> 8 Arrow\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 2 }], result: { slotId: \"arrow\", count: 8 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_pickaxe\", label: \"3 Emerald -> Stone Pickaxe\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 3 }], result: { slotId: \"stone_pickaxe\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_sliver\", label: \"5 Emerald -> 1 Sliver Ore\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 5 }], result: { slotId: \"sliver_ore\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" },\n  { id: \"trade_ruby\", label: \"8 Emerald -> 1 Ruby Ore\", cost: [{ slotId: \"emerald\", count: 8 }], result: { slotId: \"ruby_ore\", count: 1 }, station: \"villager\" }\n];\n"
}

  "new_string": "/** Opens the inventory in furnace mode so its smelting recipes unlock. */\nfunction interactFurnace(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {\n  state.inventoryOpen = true;\n  state.craftingStation = \"furnace\";\n  emit({ type: \"openedStation\", station: \"furnace\" });\n  return true;\n}\n\n/**\n * Right-click a villager to open its trades (the inventory in \"villager\" station\n * mode, which unlocks the trade offers in the recipe book). Returns true when an\n * aimed villager consumed the click. Runs after feeding in the right-click\n * precedence, but villagers aren't breedable so the two never collide.\n */\nexport function tryTradeAimedVillager(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {\n  const index = findAimedMobIndex(state);\n  if (index < 0 || state.mobs[index].kind !== \"villager\") return false;\n  state.inventoryOpen = true;\n  state.craftingStation = \"villager\";\n  emit({ type: \"openedStation\", station: \"villager\" });\n  return true;\n}"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/trades.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, test } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { createEmptySlot, createSlot, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID } from \"@/lib/game/items\";\nimport { canCraft, countsById, craft } from \"@/lib/game/inventory\";\nimport { TRADES } from \"@/lib/game/trades\";\nimport type { InventorySlot } from \"@/lib/game/types\";\n\nfunction inventory(items: Array<[string, number]>): InventorySlot[] {\n  const slots = Array.from({ length: 12 }, () => createEmptySlot());\n  items.forEach(([id, count], i) => {\n    slots[i] = createSlot(id, count);\n  });\n  return slots;\n}\n\nconst trade = (id: string) => {\n  const found = TRADES.find((t) => t.id === id);\n  if (!found) throw new Error(`trade ${id} not found`);\n  return found;\n};\n\ndescribe(\"villager trades\", () => {\n  test(\"every trade is a villager-station offer over real items\", () => {\n    for (const t of TRADES) {\n      expect(t.station).toBe(\"villager\");\n      for (const cost of t.cost) expect(ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[cost.slotId], `${t.id} cost ${cost.slotId}`).toBeDefined();\n      expect(ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[t.result.slotId], `${t.id} result ${t.result.slotId}`).toBeDefined();\n    }\n  });\n\n  test(\"the economy is two-sided: some trades pay emeralds, some spend them\", () => {\n    const earns = TRADES.filter((t) => t.result.slotId === \"emerald\");\n    const spends = TRADES.filter((t) => t.cost.some((c) => c.slotId === \"emerald\"));\n    expect(earns.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n    expect(spends.length).toBeGreaterThan(0);\n  });\n\n  test(\"selling materials yields an emerald\", () => {\n    const slots = inventory([[\"wheat\", 6]]);\n    const t = trade(\"trade_wheat\");\n    expect(canCraft(slots, t)).toBe(true);\n    expect(countsById(craft(slots, t)!).get(\"emerald\")).toBe(1);\n  });\n\n  test(\"spending an emerald buys goods\", () => {\n    const slots = inventory([[\"emerald\", 1]]);\n    const t = trade(\"trade_bread\");\n    expect(canCraft(slots, t)).toBe(true);\n    const next = craft(slots, t)!;\n    expect(countsById(next).get(\"bread\")).toBe(2);\n    expect(countsById(next).get(\"emerald\")).toBeUndefined(); // spent\n  });\n\n  test(\"a trade you can't afford is not craftable\", () => {\n    expect(canCraft(inventory([[\"emerald\", 4]]), trade(\"trade_ruby\"))).toBe(false); // needs 8\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.test.ts",
  "old_string": "  test(\"removes a creeper killed before its fuse finishes (no explosion)\", () => {\n    const creeper = makeCreeper(22.5);\n    const state = makeState([creeper]);\n    const removed: number[] = [];\n    const { deps, events } = makeDeps();\n    deps.removeMobAt = (i: number) => removed.push(i);\n\n    tickMobs(state, 0.05, deps); // primes the fuse\n    creeper.hp = 0; // killed by a sword/arrow before it detonates\n    tickMobs(state, 0.05, deps);\n\n    expect(removed).toContain(0); // swept (drops gunpowder via the engine's removeMobAt)\n    expect(events.some((e) => e.type === \"explosion\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n});",
  "new_string": "  test(\"removes a creeper killed before its fuse finishes (no explosion)\", () => {\n    const creeper = makeCreeper(22.5);\n    const state = makeState([creeper]);\n    const removed: number[] = [];\n    const { deps, events } = makeDeps();\n    deps.removeMobAt = (i: number) => removed.push(i);\n\n    tickMobs(state, 0.05, deps); // primes the fuse\n    creeper.hp = 0; // killed by a sword/arrow before it detonates\n    tickMobs(state, 0.05, deps);\n\n    expect(removed).toContain(0); // swept (drops gunpowder via the engine's removeMobAt)\n    expect(events.some((e) => e.type === \"explosion\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"villagers\", () => {\n  function makePassive(kind: MobKind, z: number): MobState {\n    const m = makeMob(kind, 24, 30, z);\n    m.hostile = false;\n    m.detectRange = 0;\n    m.speed = 0.6;\n    m.moveSpeed = 0.6;\n    return m;\n  }\n\n  test(\"a villager does not flee the player, while a sheep does\", () => {\n    // Both start one block from the player (z=24); a sheep flees, a villager holds.\n    const sheep = makePassive(\"sheep\", 23);\n    const villager = makePassive(\"villager\", 23);\n    const state = makeState([sheep, villager]);\n    const { deps } = makeDeps();\n\n    for (let i = 0; i < 12; i += 1) tickMobs(state, 0.1, deps);\n\n    const sheepDist = Math.hypot(sheep.position.x - 24, sheep.position.z - 24);\n    const villagerDist = Math.hypot(villager.position.x - 24, villager.position.z - 24);\n    expect(sheepDist).toBeGreaterThan(1.5); // the sheep backed well away\n    expect(villagerDist).toBeLessThan(sheepDist); // the villager stayed close enough to trade\n  });\n});"
}

2),\n      direction: new THREE.Vector3(0, 0, 1),\n      yaw: 0,\n      turnTimer: 9,\n      speed: 0,\n      moveSpeed: 0,\n      detectRange: 0,\n      attackDamage: 0,\n      attackCooldown: 0,\n      attackTimer: 0,\n      halfHeight: 0.9,\n      bobSeed: 0,\n      fedTimer: 0,\n      ageTimer: 0\n    });\n\n    // A villager trade is refused while no villager is open.\n    giveItem(engine, \"wheat\", 6);\n    engine.dispatch({ type: \"craft\", recipeId: \"trade_wheat\" });\n    expect(countsById(state.inventory).get(\"emerald\")).toBeUndefined();\n\n    // Right-click the villager → opens the trade station.\n    engine.consumeEvents();\n    engine.dispatch({ type: \"placeBlock\" });\n    expect(engine.consumeEvents().some((e) => e.type === \"openedStation\" && e.station === \"villager\")).toBe(true);\n    expect(state.craftingStation).toBe(\"villager\");\n    expect(state.inventoryOpen).toBe(true);\n\n    // Now the trade goes through: wheat → emerald.\n    engine.dispatch({ type: \"craft\", recipeId: \"trade_wheat\" });\n    expect(countsById(state.inventory).get(\"emerald\")).toBe(1);\n    expect(countsById(state.inventory).get(\"wheat\")).toBeUndefined();\n  });\n});"

The flee lerp is gradual (0.2/tick), so the sheep doesn't flee far in 1.2s — my distance assertion was wrong. The robust signal is the flee speed boost: a fleeing sheep gets `moveSpeed × 1.15`, a villager doesn't. Let me rewrite:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01WEZXqwqstFVPHQRqrKVCoj
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.test.ts",
  "old_string": "  test(\"a villager does not flee the player, while a sheep does\", () => {\n    // Both start one block from the player (z=24); a sheep flees, a villager holds.\n    const sheep = makePassive(\"sheep\", 23);\n    const villager = makePassive(\"villager\", 23);\n    const state = makeState([sheep, villager]);\n    const { deps } = makeDeps();\n\n    for (let i = 0; i < 12; i += 1) tickMobs(state, 0.1, deps);\n\n    const sheepDist = Math.hypot(sheep.position.x - 24, sheep.position.z - 24);\n    const villagerDist = Math.hypot(villager.position.x - 24, villager.position.z - 24);\n    expect(sheepDist).toBeGreaterThan(1.5); // the sheep backed well away\n    expect(villagerDist).toBeLessThan(sheepDist); // the villager stayed close enough to trade\n  });\n});",
  "new_string": "  test(\"a villager does not take the flee path the player triggers in a sheep\", () => {\n    // Both start two blocks from the player (well inside the 4.2 flee range). A\n    // sheep enters the flee branch (which boosts moveSpeed ×1.15 and turns away);\n    // a villager skips it and keeps its base speed, so you can walk up to trade.\n    const sheep = makePassive(\"sheep\", 22);\n    const villager = makePassive(\"villager\", 22);\n    const state = makeState([sheep, villager]);\n    const { deps } = makeDeps();\n\n    tickMobs(state, 0.05, deps);\n\n    expect(sheep.moveSpeed).toBeCloseTo(sheep.speed * 1.15, 5); // fled\n    expect(villager.moveSpeed).toBeCloseTo(villager.speed, 5); // did not flee\n  });\n});"
}

Trading is two-sided: **sell** what you gather\n(wheat, coal, leather, gold ore) for emeralds, then **spend** those emeralds on\ngoods you'd rather buy than make — bread, a stack of torches, arrows, a stone\npickaxe, even sliver or ruby ore to skip some mining. There's no limit beyond what\nyou can carry and gather, so a good farm or mine turns into steady emeralds, and\nemeralds into whatever you're short on.\n\n## Endgame: the boss"

16	## A new item or recipe
17	
18	- Add to `ITEM_DEFS` in `lib/game/items.ts` — tools take `minePower`/`mineTier`/`maxDurability`, weapons `attack`/`maxDurability`, armor `armorSlot`/`defense`/`maxDurability`. `kind: "food"` items take a `hunger` value (restored on eat); `kind: "material"` items are inert craft ingredients.
19	- Give it an inventory sprite in `lib/ui/spritePixels.ts`: pickaxes, swords, and
20	  spears get one for free when named `<material>_pickaxe`, `<material>_sword`, or
21	  `<material>_spear` and the material exists in `MATERIAL_PALETTES`;
22	  food/material items need a 16×16 grid + palette in `ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS`. The
23	  sprite integrity test rejects placeholder fallbacks.
24	- Any item with `maxDurability` is automatically non-stackable. Spears also set
25	  `meleeReach` and `throwDamage`; `systems/spears.ts` handles throwing.
26	- `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID` is derived from `ITEM_DEFS`; never edit it directly.
27	- Recipes are `{ id, label, cost: [{slotId, count}], result: {slotId, count} }` in `lib/game/recipes.ts`. An optional `station` (e.g. `"furnace"`) makes a recipe a smelting recipe: it only crafts while that station's panel is open, enforced in the `craft` command and shown locked in the recipe book.
28	- Items with durability don't stack; durability is initialized in `createSlot` and persisted in saves.
29

To open them, an interaction sets `state.craftingStation = \"villager\"` (right-clicking a villager via `tryTradeAimedVillager` in `interact.ts`); the panel then shows the trades as a **Trading** book and the `craft` command runs them. Emerald is the currency — add sell offers (material → emerald) and buy offers (emerald → goods) to keep the economy two-sided. There's no persisted trade state, so this needs no save-format change."

{

  "new_string": "### Added\n\n- **Villagers & trading**: a passive NPC you can barter with gives gathering an economy, all on **zero new assets** (procedural model + a recolored gem sprite)\n  - **Villager** mob: passive like the animals but **doesn't flee** — walk up and **right-click to trade**. It drops nothing if killed (don't murder your shopkeeper) and spawns into the day-one passive population near you\n  - **Trading**: the currency is the **emerald**. Right-clicking a villager opens its offers in the inventory's recipe book (re-titled **Trading**). The economy is two-sided — **sell** gathered materials (wheat, coal, leather, gold ore) for emeralds, then **spend** them on goods (bread, torches, arrows, a stone pickaxe, sliver/ruby ore). No use caps; trading is bounded only by what you can gather\n  - **Architecture**: a trade is simply a **`station: \"villager\"` recipe** (`lib/game/trades.ts`, spread into `RECIPES`), so it reuses the entire crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI — opening a villager just sets `craftingStation = \"villager\"`, and the existing `craft` command runs the trade. The new `\"villager\"` `MobKind` fills all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables. **No save-format or worldgen change**: villagers are transient, the open trade station is session-only, there are no per-trade use caps to persist, and emerald/traded items save by string id. (Village *structures* are a possible future addition — villagers currently wander the world among the existing houses rather than spawning in generated settlements, which keeps this change off the worldgen-determinism baseline.)\n- **Creepers, TNT & explosions**: the game gains its first explosion system"
}

{
  "command": "gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/content-expansion --title \"Content expansion: cow/pig, coal, creepers/TNT, villagers/trading\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nFour new content features that add **mechanics and creature variety** rather than more gear tiers, each on the project's **zero-binary-assets** principle (procedural textures/sprites/sounds). Committed as one focused slice per feature so they review and bisect cleanly.\n\n## What's added\n\n1. **Cow + pig** (`feat(mobs)`) — two passive farm animals following the sheep/chicken template. Cows drop leather + raw beef, pigs drop raw porkchop; both raw meats cook at a furnace and breed (cow=wheat, pig=seeds). No save/worldgen impact.\n2. **Coal & a fuel economy** (`feat(world)`) — a new shallow **coal ore** (the most common ore, wood-pickaxe mineable) drops the `coal` fuel item; **charcoal** smelts from logs as a substitute. Furnace cooking and torches now burn coal/charcoal. Coal is placed on its **own PRNG** so existing ores/trees/structures stay byte-identical — only underground coal differs. **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` 7 → 8** (affected worlds reboot from seed); save schema stays v5.\n3. **Creepers, TNT & explosions** (`feat(combat)`) — the first explosion system. `explode()` is one reusable primitive that batches over the existing block-edit chokepoint (one remesh per crater) and damages player/mobs with falloff. A **creeper** lights a fuse and detonates (dropping gunpowder; abortable by fleeing); **TNT** crafts from gunpowder + sand, ignites with a torch, and chains. Hostiles now keep a 16-block minimum spawn distance so nothing spawns point-blank. No save/worldgen change (all transient).\n4. **Villagers & an emerald trading economy** (`feat(world)`) — a passive **villager** that doesn't flee; right-click to trade. A trade is modeled as a `station: \"villager\"` recipe, so it reuses the entire crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI. The **emerald** is a two-sided currency (sell materials → emeralds → buy goods). No save/worldgen change. (Village *structures* are deferred to keep this off the worldgen-determinism baseline — no second version bump.)\n\n## Verification\n\nAll green: `bun run lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `bun test` (540 tests), `bun run build`, and `bun run test:e2e` (14 Playwright smoke tests). Each phase ships with tests (new `explosion.test.ts`, `trades.test.ts`, `mining.test.ts`; extended mob/recipe/breeding/engine tests) and docs (reference, manual, tuning, save-format, testing, adding-content) plus CHANGELOG entries.\n\n## Notes\n\n- One `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump total (coal); it discards affected worlds' block-diffs, rebooting them from seed. The save *schema* stays version 5 throughout.\n- Two new `MobKind`s (creeper, villager) — each fills all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables (typecheck-enforced).\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open PR against hutusi/main"
}

Islands spawn de-risked: islands keeps land gently sloped so findSpawnOnLand lands dry (engine test asserts it). > AGENT

Done. Here's the wrap-up.

## Your question, answered

You don't need *more gear* — the game already has 8 sword tiers, 7 pickaxe tiers, 5 ore tiers, a boss, farming, breeding, dungeons. What it was missing was **mechanics and creatures**. So I skipped the redundant suggestions (Iron Ore already exists as **Sliver Ore**; Crafting Tables don't fit since crafting is intentionally always-on) and built the four high-leverage additions you picked.

## What shipped — PR [hutusi/monecraft#23](https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/pull/23)

One branch, one PR, one focused commit per feature:

| Commit | Feature |
|---|---|
| `feat(mobs)` | **Cow + pig** — beef/porkchop/leather, cook recipes, breeding |
| `feat(world)` | **Coal & a fuel economy** — shallow coal ore, charcoal, coal-gated torches |
| `feat(combat)` | **Creepers, TNT & explosions** — one reusable `explode()` primitive, fuses, chaining, + a 16-block minimum hostile spawn distance |
| `feat(world)` | **Villagers & emerald trading** — a two-sided economy that reuses the crafting UI |

## Verification — all green

`lint` · `typecheck` · `format:check` · **540 unit/integration tests** · `build` · **14 Playwright e2e**. Each phase shipped with its own tests (`explosion.test.ts`, `trades.test.ts`, `mining.test.ts`, plus extended mob/recipe/breeding/engine tests) and full docs + CHANGELOG entries.

## Two decisions worth flagging

1. **One `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump (7→8)**, from coal only — placed on its own PRNG so existing ores/trees/structures stay byte-identical; only underground coal differs. Affected worlds reboot from seed; the save *schema* stays v5 throughout.
2. **Village *structures* deferred.** Building generated settlements would have forced a *second*, surface-altering worldgen re-baseline — the riskiest part of the plan. I scoped Phase 4 to the villager mob + the full trading economy (the actual fun), with villagers wandering among the existing ~120 houses. That kept the whole feature off the determinism baseline. It's noted in the PR and CHANGELOG as a clean future addition if you want real villages later.

**One thing left for you:** a manual visual pass — the creeper swell/explosion particles, TNT crater, and the trade panel can't be verified headless. Everything else is automated and green.

> DEVELOPER

=== lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts:264 ===
_⚠️ Potential issue_ | _🟠 Major_ | _⚡ Quick win_

**Tick primed TNT during the dead-state branch.**

`tickPrimedTnt` runs only in the alive path (Line 263), so fuses freeze for the full death countdown. This makes TNT timing inconsistent and can preserve lit TNT unexpectedly until after respawn.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
     if (state.isDead) {
       if (tickRespawnTimer(state, dt)) this.respawn();
       else {
         tickMobs(state, dt, this.mobTickDeps);
+        tickPrimedTnt(state, dt, this.mobTickDeps);
         // Keep ticking so in-flight arrows clear; applyDamage no-ops while dead.
         tickProjectiles(state, dt, this.mobTickDeps);
       }
       this.refreshSnapshot();
       return;
     }
```
</details>

<details>
<summary>🤖 Prompt for AI Agents</summary>

```
Verify each finding against current code. Fix only still-valid issues, skip the
rest with a brief reason, keep changes minimal, and validate.

In `@lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` around lines 263 - 264, The tickPrimedTnt
function call at line 263 only executes in the alive path, causing TNT fuses to
freeze during the death countdown and resulting in inconsistent TNT timing. Move
or duplicate the tickPrimedTnt call so it executes in both the alive path and
the dead-state branch (the conditional block that handles the dead state). This
ensures TNT fuses continue to tick down consistently regardless of whether the
player is alive or dead.
```

</details>

> AGENT

The CodeRabbit review on PR #23 is in. Here's the rundown and what I did.

## CodeRabbit's findings (4 total)

| # | Finding | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **TNT fuse freezes during the death countdown** — `tickPrimedTnt` ran only in the alive path, so a lit TNT would pause until after respawn (creeper fuses already tick during death). | ✅ **Fixed** — added `tickPrimedTnt` to the dead-state branch. `applyDamage` no-ops while dead, so no double-kill. |
| 2 | **Dead mob acts before the sweep** — a mob reduced to 0 hp by a blast earlier in the tick still ran its full AI (could land a final hit) before the post-loop removal. | ✅ **Fixed** — `if (mob.hp <= 0) continue;` at the top of the mob loop. Added a regression test. |
| 3 | **Creeper fuse can arm without a LOS raycast** — a creeper in fuse range but with a large vertical gap isn't `meleeReady`, so it armed off the default `hasLineOfSight = true` and could explode through a thin floor. | ✅ **Fixed** — added `creeperFuseReady` to the LOS-computation condition and the arming check. |
| 4 | **Replace `as unknown as GameState` in the new tests with a strict factory.** | ⏭️ **Skipped** — every existing system test (combat, breeding, mobAI) uses that exact partial-state pattern; matching it keeps the new tests consistent. Noted the reason in the commit. |

All three are genuine (if minor) correctness issues, all low-risk. The overall review was `COMMENTED` (not requesting changes) — no blocking objections, no human reviewers yet.

## Status

Fixes committed locally as `709e106`, full gate re-run green: lint · typecheck · format · **541 tests** · build. **I have not pushed** — per your usual flow I'm holding it so you can check the diff first.

## Summary

**[PR #23](https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/pull/23) is merged** into `hutusi/main` — merge commit `8079b53`, feature branch deleted, local `main` synced.

The full content expansion is now on `main`:
- **Cow + pig** — farm animals with beef/porkchop/leather, cooking, breeding
- **Coal & fuel economy** — shallow coal ore, charcoal, coal-gated torches
- **Creepers, TNT & explosions** — a reusable explosion primitive, fuses, chaining, minimum spawn distance
- **Villagers & emerald trading** — a two-sided economy reusing the crafting UI

Plus the **CodeRabbit review fixes** (TNT-during-death, dead-mob skip, creeper LOS gate). Final state: 5 commits, all CI green (`verify` + `e2e` + CodeRabbit), 541 tests + 14 e2e passing, one `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump (coal), no save-schema change.

Two open follow-ups, neither blocking:
- **Manual visual pass** — the creeper swell/explosion particles, TNT crater, and trade panel can't be verified headless.
- It's all still under `[Unreleased]` — no version bump/tag was cut (consistent with your recent per-feature → batched-release approach). Say the word if you want me to roll a release (package.json bump + roll the changelog + tag).
